---
title: Adipose Endocrine Regulation
pm_id: BRS6-FM4-PM8
evidence_gaps_tab: true
evidence_tabs_version: 1
scientific_findings_placement: section-4
open_issues_policy: evidence-gaps-only
evidence_gaps:
  - id: PM8-EG-1
    title: Dietary systemic cortisol changes do not establish local adipose target engagement
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F13
    citation_keys:
      - stimson_diet_cortisol_2007
      - stomby_diet_glucocorticoid_2015
    establishes: >-
      Human tracer studies distinguish local adipose cortisol regeneration from whole-body metabolism. The
      dietary crossover changed whole-body cortisol metabolism without a corresponding change in measured
      subcutaneous adipose 11β-HSD1. Separate long-term dietary evidence measured decreased adipose 11β-HSD1
      mRNA, not direct enzyme activity; this qualifies a blanket claim of no local dietary response.
    cannot_establish: That the tested diet changes the local adipose glucocorticoid operation defined by PM8.
    missing_link: >-
      Systemic cortisol metabolism is not a measurement of local adipose enzyme activity. Depot and
      measurement context remain material.
    required_evidence: >-
      Dietary studies measuring relevant local adipose glucocorticoid target engagement alongside systemic
      endpoints.
    affected_relationship: >-
      Activity/flux and tissue specificity of dietary glucocorticoid regulation; local transcript responses
      retained without inferring enzyme activity.
  - id: PM8-EG-2
    title: Mouse adipocyte-receptor effects do not establish a human dietary stress-feedback route
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F14
    citation_keys:
      - de_kloet_adipocyte_gr_2015
    establishes: >-
      Adipocyte glucocorticoid-receptor reduction altered stress-feedback responses in a genetic mouse
      experiment.
    cannot_establish: >-
      An identified adipokine mediator, an equivalent human pathway or a human dietary intervention acting
      through that pathway.
    missing_link: >-
      The signalling mediator remains unresolved; genetic perturbation in mice is not human dietary target
      engagement.
    required_evidence: >-
      Identification of the mediator and appropriate human pathway and intervention measurements before a
      dietary claim is supported.
    affected_relationship: Adipocyte glucocorticoid-to-brain stress feedback.
  - id: PM8-EG-3
    title: DHA food composition does not establish supplement-equivalent local secretion
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F18
      - PM8-F19
    citation_keys: &ref_5
      - tishinsky_epa_adiponectin_2011
      - wu_fish_oil_adiponectin_2013
    establishes: >-
      Defined oral DHA can modulate circulating adiponectin; mixed fish oil can be incorporated into adipose
      tissue. Human cell experiments separately measured secretion.
    cannot_establish: >-
      That ordinary DHA-containing foods reproduce trial formulations, or that the circulating response proves
      local secretion or receptor sensitivity.
    missing_link: >-
      Food-to-formulation equivalence and the local secretory mediator remain unestablished; this is not a
      general failure of oral delivery.
    required_evidence: >-
      A defined dietary exposure with relevant adipose exposure and endocrine engagement measurements,
      retaining formulation and population boundaries.
    affected_relationship: >-
      The bounded DHA Type B circulating-signal admission is retained; stronger food-efficacy or
      local-secretory claims remain unsupported.
  - id: PM8-EG-4
    title: Local exercise responses do not establish a universal secretion or benefit claim
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F17
    citation_keys: &ref_0
      - hojbjerre_exercise_adiponectin_2007
      - moghadasi_endurance_adiponectin_2012
      - polak_aerobic_adipokines_2006
    establishes: >-
      Some aerobic protocols altered local interstitial hormone concentration or adipose transcripts; another
      training context was null.
    cannot_establish: 'A universal secretion-rate response, optimal training dose or improved receptor sensitivity.'
    missing_link: 'Local concentration, transcript, release flux and clinical outcomes are separate endpoints.'
    required_evidence: >-
      Comparable protocols with prespecified secretion/flux or responsiveness endpoints and energy-balance
      control.
    affected_relationship: The admitted aerobic practice is limited to context-specific local endocrine engagement.
  - id: PM8-EG-5
    title: Flaxseed trial effects do not identify an active constituent or universal response
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F20
    citation_keys:
      - motlagh_flaxseed_adiponectin_2021
      - abbasi_flaxseed_adipokines_2023
    establishes: >-
      A defined milled-food trial reported a circulating adiponectin difference; pooled evidence was
      heterogeneous and not significant overall.
    cannot_establish: >-
      An isolated ALA, lignan or fibre effect, a weight-independent secretory mechanism or a universal
      recommendation.
    missing_link: Component attribution and reproducibility across populations/forms remain unresolved.
    required_evidence: Comparable food protocols with appropriate weight mediation and endocrine mechanism measurements.
    affected_relationship: Bounded PM8-SOP-2; no isolated-substance admission.
  - id: PM8-EG-6
    title: Soluble-fibre endocrine claim lacks coherent overall trial support
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F21
    citation_keys:
      - zeinabi_soluble_fibre_adipokines_2023
    establishes: Pooled circulating results include no-effect values and substantial heterogeneity.
    cannot_establish: A generic soluble-fibre intervention for PM8 endocrine regulation.
    missing_link: Specific preparation/exposure and coherent endocrine engagement remain unestablished.
    required_evidence: Subtype- and population-specific trials with prespecified relevant endpoints and reproducible effects.
    affected_relationship: No general fibre Type A/B admission.
mission: >-
  Regulate adipose-derived endocrine signalling and local glucocorticoid responsiveness to communicate
  metabolic state and coordinate hormonal adaptation.
parent_fm: BRS6(FM4)
parent_brs: BRS6
summary: >-
  Adipose tissue releases hormones that communicate metabolic state. Adipokine output and local glucocorticoid
  responsiveness are regulated processes whose measured endpoints must be distinguished.
key_constraints: []
cofactors: []
intervention_dominance: Diet-Supported
intervention_breakdown: Diet-Supported
timing_specific: 'No'
dose_sensitivity: 'Effects depend on the tested exposure, preparation and study population; no universal dose is established.'

scientific_findings:
  - id: PM8-F12
    finding_label: 'Adipokine responses depend on hormone, depot and exposure'
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Insulin and glucocorticoid exposures regulate leptin synthesis or release and adiponectin expression or
      release in human adipose preparations, with context-dependent responses.
    finding_statement: >-
      Insulin and glucocorticoid exposures regulate leptin synthesis or release and adiponectin expression or
      release in human adipose preparations, with context-dependent responses.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      Human adipose pulse-labelling separates leptin biosynthesis from release of stored protein.
      Dexamethasone can increase leptin synthesis while suppressing adiponectin release in other preparations;
      these are distinct hormone-specific responses. Circulating adiponectin responses to systemic
      glucocorticoids are not uniformly reproduced.
    synthesis: >-
      Human adipose pulse-labelling separates leptin biosynthesis from release of stored protein.
      Dexamethasone can increase leptin synthesis while suppressing adiponectin release in other preparations;
      these are distinct hormone-specific responses. Circulating adiponectin responses to systemic
      glucocorticoids are not uniformly reproduced.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Ex vivo hormone exposures and pharmacological glucocorticoids do not establish ordinary dietary effects.
      Donor adiposity, depot, exposure duration and concentration matter; circulating concentrations do not
      measure secretion rates or receptor sensitivity.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Lee et al. (2007)
        citation_key: lee_leptin_adipose_2007
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#lee_leptin_adipose_2007
        doi: 10.1152/ajpendo.00439.2006
        canonical_reference:
          author_year: Lee et al. (2007)
          number: 30
          anchor: '#pm-ref-30'
        data_level: Human Mechanistic
        evidence_source: bounded-external-search
        publication_class: primary
        study_exposure: >-
          Human adipose organ culture; acute versus 1–2-day insulin/dexamethasone exposure and metabolic
          pulse-chase labelling.
        study_finding: >-
          Chronic insulin increased leptin biosynthesis without a matching mRNA rise; dexamethasone increased
          mRNA and biosynthesis. Acute insulin increased release of labelled preformed leptin.
        directional_finding: >-
          Chronic insulin increased leptin biosynthesis without a matching mRNA rise; dexamethasone increased
          mRNA and biosynthesis. Acute insulin increased release of labelled preformed leptin.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            Human adipose organ culture; acute versus 1–2-day insulin/dexamethasone exposure and metabolic
            pulse-chase labelling.
          population: >-
            Human adipose organ culture; acute versus 1–2-day insulin/dexamethasone exposure and metabolic
            pulse-chase labelling.
          result: >-
            Chronic insulin increased leptin biosynthesis without a matching mRNA rise; dexamethasone
            increased mRNA and biosynthesis. Acute insulin increased release of labelled preformed leptin.
          effect_magnitude: >-
            Abstract reports nearly doubled labelled leptin release after a one-hour chase; no exact
            uncertainty recovered.
          evidence_summary: >-
            Chronic insulin increased leptin biosynthesis without a matching mRNA rise; dexamethasone
            increased mRNA and biosynthesis. Acute insulin increased release of labelled preformed leptin.
          limitations: >-
            Ex vivo exposure; donor and assay-level bias/precision require full-method appraisal. No food
            comparison.
          retrieval_scope: >-
            Primary abstract/metadata; accessible journal excerpts where available. Complete methods and
            numerical tables not independently recovered.
          source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1152/ajpendo.00439.2006'
      - label: Degawa-Yamauchi et al. (2005)
        citation_key: degawa_adiponectin_2005
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#degawa_adiponectin_2005
        doi: 10.1038/oby.2005.74
        canonical_reference:
          author_year: Degawa-Yamauchi et al. (2005)
          number: 31
          anchor: '#pm-ref-31'
        data_level: Human Mechanistic
        evidence_source: bounded-external-search
        publication_class: primary
        study_exposure: 'Isolated human subcutaneous and omental adipocytes, with dexamethasone and TNFα incubation.'
        study_finding: >-
          Dexamethasone inhibited adiponectin release after 24 hours; TNFα response varied with donor
          adiposity and depot.
        directional_finding: >-
          Dexamethasone inhibited adiponectin release after 24 hours; TNFα response varied with donor
          adiposity and depot.
        assessment:

  - id: PM8-F13
    finding_label: Local adipose cortisol regeneration differs from systemic metabolism
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Human adipose cortisol regeneration is tissue- and metabolic-context dependent; whole-body dietary
      changes in cortisol metabolism do not necessarily alter subcutaneous adipose 11β-HSD1.
    finding_statement: >-
      Human adipose cortisol regeneration is tissue- and metabolic-context dependent; whole-body dietary
      changes in cortisol metabolism do not necessarily alter subcutaneous adipose 11β-HSD1.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      Microdialysis and tracer measurements distinguish local cortisone-to-cortisol conversion from systemic
      turnover. Insulin effects differed by adiposity. A low-carbohydrate crossover altered systemic cortisol
      metabolism without a measured adipose 11β-HSD1 response; weight loss in another small study increased
      adipocyte transcript expression rather than demonstrating universal suppression.
    synthesis: >-
      Microdialysis and tracer measurements distinguish local cortisone-to-cortisol conversion from systemic
      turnover. Insulin effects differed by adiposity. A low-carbohydrate crossover altered systemic cortisol
      metabolism without a measured adipose 11β-HSD1 response; weight loss in another small study increased
      adipocyte transcript expression rather than demonstrating universal suppression.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Small selected samples and differing assays; transcript abundance is not in vivo regeneration flux.
      Systemic or hepatic effects cannot be assigned to adipose, and less local cortisol activity is not
      assumed universally beneficial.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Sandeep et al. (2005)
        citation_key: sandeep_adipose_cortisol_2005
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#sandeep_adipose_cortisol_2005
        doi: 10.2337/diabetes.54.3.872
        canonical_reference:
          author_year: Sandeep et al. (2005)
          number: 29
          anchor: '#pm-ref-29'
        data_level: Human Mechanistic
        evidence_source: bounded-external-search
        publication_class: primary
        study_exposure: >-
          Six lean and six obese men; cortisol tracer, adipose microdialysis and carbenoxolone crossover in
          obese men.
        study_finding: >-
          Adipose cortisone conversion was greater in obese men although whole-body regeneration did not
          differ. Insulin suppressed adipose activity in lean but not obese men; carbenoxolone did not
          significantly inhibit adipose activity or improve insulin sensitivity.
        directional_finding: >-
          Adipose cortisone conversion was greater in obese men although whole-body regeneration did not
          differ. Insulin suppressed adipose activity in lean but not obese men; carbenoxolone did not
          significantly inhibit adipose activity or improve insulin sensitivity.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            Six lean and six obese men; cortisol tracer, adipose microdialysis and carbenoxolone crossover in
            obese men.
          population: >-
            Six lean and six obese men; cortisol tracer, adipose microdialysis and carbenoxolone crossover in
            obese men.
          result: >-
            Adipose cortisone conversion was greater in obese men although whole-body regeneration did not
            differ. Insulin suppressed adipose activity in lean but not obese men; carbenoxolone did not
            significantly inhibit adipose activity or improve insulin sensitivity.
          effect_magnitude: Direction and endpoint retained; no unverified effect size or uncertainty estimate supplied.
          evidence_summary: >-
            Adipose cortisone conversion was greater in obese men although whole-body regeneration did not
            differ. Insulin suppressed adipose activity in lean but not obese men; carbenoxolone did not
            significantly inhibit adipose activity or improve insulin sensitivity.
          limitations: Small male sample; non-selective pharmacology and distinct systemic versus local assays.
          retrieval_scope: >-

          source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.2337/diabetes.54.3.872'

  - id: PM8-F14
    finding_label: Adipocyte glucocorticoid-receptor reduction changes mouse stress feedback
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Reducing glucocorticoid-receptor expression in adiponectin-Cre mice increased restraint-related
      corticosterone responses and impaired dexamethasone feedback, without a detected baseline leptin or
      adiponectin difference.
    finding_statement: >-
      Reducing glucocorticoid-receptor expression in adiponectin-Cre mice increased restraint-related
      corticosterone responses and impaired dexamethasone feedback, without a detected baseline leptin or
      adiponectin difference.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      The genetic experiment supports a qualified contribution of adipocyte-enriched GR signalling to mouse
      stress feedback. The communicating signal was not identified; unchanged baseline adipokines do not
      establish an adipokine-mediated pathway. Human stress feedback through the same route remains
      unresolved.
    synthesis: >-
      The genetic experiment supports a qualified contribution of adipocyte-enriched GR signalling to mouse
      stress feedback. The communicating signal was not identified; unchanged baseline adipokines do not
      establish an adipokine-mediated pathway. Human stress feedback through the same route remains
      unresolved.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Genetic mouse model, incomplete and depot-variable knockdown, possible untested off-target sites, and
      developmental effects. Baseline hormone concentrations do not exclude dynamic secretion changes. No
      human dietary, cognitive or ADHD effect is established.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: de Kloet et al. (2015)
        citation_key: de_kloet_adipocyte_gr_2015
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#de_kloet_adipocyte_gr_2015
        doi: 10.1016/j.psyneuen.2015.03.008
        canonical_reference:
          author_year: de Kloet et al. (2015)
          number: 25
          anchor: '#pm-ref-25'
        data_level: Animal Data
        evidence_source: bounded-external-search
        publication_class: primary
        study_exposure: >-
          Adiponectin-promoter Cre/lox GR knockdown and littermate controls; restraint, dexamethasone
          suppression, ACTH challenge, expression and circulating hormones.
        study_finding: >-
          Stress corticosterone increased and suppression was impaired; tested central/adrenal GR expression
          and adrenal ACTH responsiveness did not explain the result. Baseline leptin and adiponectin did not
          differ.
        directional_finding: >-
          Stress corticosterone increased and suppression was impaired; tested central/adrenal GR expression
          and adrenal ACTH responsiveness did not explain the result. Baseline leptin and adiponectin did not
          differ.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            Adiponectin-promoter Cre/lox GR knockdown and littermate controls; restraint, dexamethasone
            suppression, ACTH challenge, expression and circulating hormones.
          population: >-
            Adiponectin-promoter Cre/lox GR knockdown and littermate controls; restraint, dexamethasone
            suppression, ACTH challenge, expression and circulating hormones.
          result: >-
            Stress corticosterone increased and suppression was impaired; tested central/adrenal GR expression
            and adrenal ACTH responsiveness did not explain the result. Baseline leptin and adiponectin did
            not differ.
          effect_magnitude: >-
            Male restraint AUC t(67)=4.614, p<0.01; dexamethasone experiment n=5/group. These are distinct
            comparisons, not independent replications.
          evidence_summary: >-
            Stress corticosterone increased and suppression was impaired; tested central/adrenal GR expression
            and adrenal ACTH responsiveness did not explain the result. Baseline leptin and adiponectin did
            not differ.
          limitations: >-
            Adiponectin-Cre specificity not exhaustive; knockdown varied by depot, mediator unresolved, and no
            human intervention.

  - id: PM8-F15
    finding_label: Diet and activity outcomes distinguish circulating hormones from local production
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Dietary energy restriction and physical activity can change selected circulating adipokines or adipose
      transcripts, but these endpoints do not establish a uniform change in adipokine secretion or improved
      endocrine responsiveness.
    finding_statement: >-
      Dietary energy restriction and physical activity can change selected circulating adipokines or adipose
      transcripts, but these endpoints do not establish a uniform change in adipokine secretion or improved
      endocrine responsiveness.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      Alternate-day and daily energy restriction reduced circulating leptin without an adiponectin change in
      one randomised trial. POUNDS Lost reported increased circulating adiponectin over two years across diet
      groups. A small walking trial increased adipose adiponectin mRNA without a significant plasma increase.
      Duration, tissue measurement and circulating exposure must remain separate.
    synthesis: >-
      Alternate-day and daily energy restriction reduced circulating leptin without an adiponectin change in
      one randomised trial. POUNDS Lost reported increased circulating adiponectin over two years across diet
      groups. A small walking trial increased adipose adiponectin mRNA without a significant plasma increase.
      Duration, tissue measurement and circulating exposure must remain separate.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      These trials do not directly quantify adipose secretion or receptor sensitivity. Lower leptin or higher
      adiponectin is not automatically a better endocrine state. Selected populations, adherence and weight
      change limit translation; no optimal universal protocol or brain outcome is established.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Trepanowski et al. (2018; online 2017)
        citation_key: trepanowski_adipokines_2018

        study_exposure: >-
          Pre-planned secondary analysis of 100 adults randomised to alternate-day restriction, daily
          restriction or control for 24 weeks.
        study_finding: >-
          Leptin decreased in both restriction groups; circulating adiponectin, resistin, IL-6 and TNFα did
          not change relative to control.
        directional_finding: >-
          Leptin decreased in both restriction groups; circulating adiponectin, resistin, IL-6 and TNFα did
          not change relative to control.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            Pre-planned secondary analysis of 100 adults randomised to alternate-day restriction, daily
            restriction or control for 24 weeks.
          population: >-
            Pre-planned secondary analysis of 100 adults randomised to alternate-day restriction, daily
            restriction or control for 24 weeks.
          result: >-
            Leptin decreased in both restriction groups; circulating adiponectin, resistin, IL-6 and TNFα did
            not change relative to control.
          effect_magnitude: Direction and endpoint retained; no unverified effect size or uncertainty estimate supplied.
          evidence_summary: >-
            Leptin decreased in both restriction groups; circulating adiponectin, resistin, IL-6 and TNFα did
            not change relative to control.
          limitations: >-
            Secondary endpoints; circulating concentrations, not secretion or sensitivity. No superiority of

          retrieval_scope: >-

  - id: PM8-F16
    finding_label: Dietary weight-reduction programmes alter local adipokine release within studied contexts
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Hypocaloric weight-reduction programmes in women with obesity reduced ex vivo adipose leptin release,
      while adiponectin responses were depot-specific or unchanged.
    finding_statement: >-
      Hypocaloric weight-reduction programmes in women with obesity reduced ex vivo adipose leptin release,
      while adiponectin responses were depot-specific or unchanged.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      Independent programmes measured adipose release rather than relying on circulating concentrations alone.
      You measured release in 27 of 42 women and found abdominal but not gluteal adiponectin increases.
      Arvidsson found lower leptin secretion without an adiponectin increase. Siklova shows that active
      restriction and stabilisation differ. Fasting and mixed-meal arteriovenous studies provide contextual
      evidence of acute local regulation, not equivalent weight-loss protocols.
    synthesis: >-
      Independent programmes measured adipose release rather than relying on circulating concentrations alone.
      You measured release in 27 of 42 women and found abdominal but not gluteal adiponectin increases.
      Arvidsson found lower leptin secretion without an adiponectin increase. Siklova shows that active
      restriction and stabilisation differ. Fasting and mixed-meal arteriovenous studies provide contextual
      evidence of acute local regulation, not equivalent weight-loss protocols.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Energy deficit, weight loss and co-interventions are not fully separable. Ex vivo release,
      venous–arterial concentration difference, transcript and plasma measurements remain distinct. No
      universal optimal hormone direction, food attribution, brain benefit or weight-independent endocrine
      effect is established.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: You et al. (2014)
        citation_key: you_regional_release_2014
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#you_regional_release_2014

          anchor: '#pm-ref-39'
        data_level: Human Mechanistic
        evidence_source: bounded-external-search
        publication_class: primary
        study_exposure: >-
          42 older abdominally obese women; 20-week hypocaloric programme, with or without aerobic exercise;
          pooled pre/post analysis. Ex vivo release measured in 27 women.
        study_finding: >-
          Weight loss 10.1 ± 0.7 kg. Leptin release decreased in both depots; adiponectin release increased
          only abdominally. Tissue and circulating changes were not significantly correlated.
        directional_finding: >-
          Weight loss 10.1 ± 0.7 kg. Leptin release decreased in both depots; adiponectin release increased
          only abdominally. Tissue and circulating changes were not significantly correlated.
        assessment:

          result: >-

            Weight loss 10.1 ± 0.7 kg. Leptin release decreased in both depots; adiponectin release increased

  - id: PM8-F17
    finding_label: Aerobic exercise engages local adipokine regulation with compartment-specific and null responses
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Aerobic exercise changed adipose interstitial adiponectin or adipokine transcripts in some human
      protocols, with null tissue or circulating responses in other studied populations.
    finding_statement: >-
      Aerobic exercise changed adipose interstitial adiponectin or adipokine transcripts in some human
      protocols, with null tissue or circulating responses in other studied populations.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      Acute exercise increased local interstitial adiponectin while plasma stayed unchanged and mRNA
      decreased. A small training study increased adipose adiponectin mRNA; another training study found no
      changes in tested tissue transcripts. These are different measurements and contexts, not independent
      replications of one secretion-rate effect.
    synthesis: >-
      Acute exercise increased local interstitial adiponectin while plasma stayed unchanged and mRNA
      decreased. A small training study increased adipose adiponectin mRNA; another training study found no
      changes in tested tissue transcripts. These are different measurements and contexts, not independent
      replications of one secretion-rate effect.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Interstitial concentration is not secretion flux, and mRNA is not released protein. Training-related
      adiposity changes, small biopsy samples and sex/protocol differences limit generalisation. Neither an
      optimal exercise dose nor improved adipokine receptor sensitivity, brain function or clinical benefit is
      established.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Højbjerre et al. (2007)

          Interstitial adiponectin increased despite unchanged plasma adiponectin and decreased adiponectin

        directional_finding: >-
          Interstitial adiponectin increased despite unchanged plasma adiponectin and decreased adiponectin

        assessment:

          population: >-

          result: >-

            mRNA. Leptin mRNA decreased.

            Only the explicitly verified numerical results in this source assessment are claimed; no

          evidence_summary: >-
            Interstitial adiponectin increased despite unchanged plasma adiponectin and decreased adiponectin
            mRNA. Leptin mRNA decreased.
          limitations: >-

  - id: PM8-F18
    finding_label: EPA/DHA adipocyte secretion effects do not establish equivalent food delivery
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Defined EPA and DHA exposures increased adiponectin secretion in cultured human adipocytes; oral
      fish-oil trials show heterogeneous circulating responses without establishing the same local secretory
      mechanism.
    finding_statement: >-
      Defined EPA and DHA exposures increased adiponectin secretion in cultured human adipocytes; oral
      fish-oil trials show heterogeneous circulating responses without establishing the same local secretory
      mechanism.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      The cell experiment used 100 µM free fatty acid for 48 hours and measured secreted protein. The fish-oil
      meta-analysis concerns circulating concentrations, includes substantial heterogeneity and loses a clear
      effect after publication-bias adjustment; fish-feeding evidence did not establish an increase. The
      secondary synthesis is not an independent cell experiment.
    synthesis: >-
      The cell experiment used 100 µM free fatty acid for 48 hours and measured secreted protein. The fish-oil
      meta-analysis concerns circulating concentrations, includes substantial heterogeneity and loses a clear
      effect after publication-bias adjustment; fish-feeding evidence did not establish an increase. The
      secondary synthesis is not an independent cell experiment.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      No equivalence between cultured exposure, supplement formulations and ordinary fish foods is
      established. PPARγ and drug-combination results do not establish dietary synergy. A missing essential
      delivery-and-engagement bridge is not repaired by a Low confidence label.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Tishinsky et al. (2011)
        citation_key: tishinsky_epa_adiponectin_2011
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#tishinsky_epa_adiponectin_2011

        publication_class: primary
        study_exposure: >-
          Isolated cultured human adipocytes exposed for 48 hours to 100 µM free EPA or DHA, with PPARγ
          perturbation and rosiglitazone comparisons.
        study_finding: Adiponectin secretion rose 88% with EPA and 47% with DHA; antagonist responses differed.
        directional_finding: Adiponectin secretion rose 88% with EPA and 47% with DHA; antagonist responses differed.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            Isolated cultured human adipocytes exposed for 48 hours to 100 µM free EPA or DHA, with PPARγ
            perturbation and rosiglitazone comparisons.
          population: >-
            Isolated cultured human adipocytes exposed for 48 hours to 100 µM free EPA or DHA, with PPARγ
            perturbation and rosiglitazone comparisons.
          result: Adiponectin secretion rose 88% with EPA and 47% with DHA; antagonist responses differed.
          effect_magnitude: >-
            Only the explicitly verified numerical results in this source assessment are claimed; no
            unverified confidence interval or food-effective dose is supplied.
          evidence_summary: Adiponectin secretion rose 88% with EPA and 47% with DHA; antagonist responses differed.
          limitations: >-
            Cultured free-fatty-acid exposure is not dietary delivery. Rosiglitazone is pharmacological; no
            ordinary-fish, food-dose or clinical equivalence.
          retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
          source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1038/oby.2010.186'

  - id: PM8-F19
    finding_label: DHA formulations can alter circulating adiponectin in defined human contexts
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      Defined oral DHA exposures altered circulating adiponectin in selected human intervention contexts;
      mixed marine-omega-3 and enriched-oil responses do not establish adipose secretion or ordinary-food
      equivalence.
    finding_statement: >-
      Defined oral DHA exposures altered circulating adiponectin in selected human intervention contexts;
      mixed marine-omega-3 and enriched-oil responses do not establish adipose secretion or ordinary-food
      equivalence.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      The DHA-versus-EPA crossover provides substance-specific evidence of a circulating adipose-derived
      endocrine signal response. Gammelmark demonstrates oral mixed-fatty-acid delivery and adipose
      incorporation; Baril-Gravel tests an enriched oil diet, not isolated DHA. Rausch preserves heterogeneous
      and null trials rather than counting favourable directions as proof. Circulating signal modulation is
      the admitted scope; synthesis, secretion and receptor responsiveness remain unmeasured.
    synthesis: >-
      The DHA-versus-EPA crossover provides substance-specific evidence of a circulating adipose-derived
      endocrine signal response. Gammelmark demonstrates oral mixed-fatty-acid delivery and adipose
      incorporation; Baril-Gravel tests an enriched oil diet, not isolated DHA. Rausch preserves heterogeneous
      and null trials rather than counting favourable directions as proof. Circulating signal modulation is
      the admitted scope; synthesis, secretion and receptor responsiveness remain unmeasured.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Supplement/enriched-oil exposures, selected populations and heterogeneous trials limit extrapolation. No
      universal benefit from higher adiponectin, isolated EPA effect, ordinary-fish efficacy, effective food
      dose or brain/ADHD effect established. Some primary full methods and supplements remain unavailable;
      review-table bias judgments are not independent primary verification.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Allaire et al. (2016)
        citation_key: allaire_compared_2016
        doi: 10.3945/ajcn.116.131896
        href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#allaire_compared_2016
        canonical_reference:
          author_year: Allaire et al. (2016)
          number: 68
          anchor: '#pm-ref-68'
        publication_class: primary
        data_level: Human Study
        evidence_source: bounded-external-search
        study_exposure: >-
          154 adults randomised in double-blind crossover; 2.7 g/day EPA, 2.7 g/day DHA (90%-pure
          re-esterified triglyceride supplements), or corn oil for 10 weeks, 9-week washouts.
        study_finding: >-
          DHA increased adiponectin more than EPA: +3.1% ±1.6% versus −1.2% ±1.7%, P<0.001 in primary
          abstract; Rausch reports DHA-control contrast.
        directional_finding: >-
          DHA increased adiponectin more than EPA: +3.1% ±1.6% versus −1.2% ±1.7%, P<0.001 in primary
          abstract; Rausch reports DHA-control contrast.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            154 adults randomised in double-blind crossover; 2.7 g/day EPA, 2.7 g/day DHA (90%-pure
            re-esterified triglyceride supplements), or corn oil for 10 weeks, 9-week washouts.
          population: >-
            154 adults randomised in double-blind crossover; 2.7 g/day EPA, 2.7 g/day DHA (90%-pure
            re-esterified triglyceride supplements), or corn oil for 10 weeks, 9-week washouts.
          result: >-
            DHA increased adiponectin more than EPA: +3.1% ±1.6% versus −1.2% ±1.7%, P<0.001 in primary
            abstract; Rausch reports DHA-control contrast.
          effect_magnitude: >-
            DHA increased adiponectin more than EPA: +3.1% ±1.6% versus −1.2% ±1.7%, P<0.001 in primary
            abstract; Rausch reports DHA-control contrast.
          evidence_summary: >-
            DHA-specific oral exposure and endocrine signal modulation support the bounded Type B proposition.
            This is not evidence for tissue secretion or general improvement.
          limitations: >-
            No adipose secretion/receptor assay; selected population and supplement form; no ordinary-food,
            dose-equivalence or clinical benefit inferred.
          retrieval_scope: Primary abstract/metadata recovered; publisher full text and supplements not recovered.

  - id: PM8-F20
    finding_label: Milled flaxseed has a bounded circulating adiponectin trial signal
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      A 12-week milled-flaxseed trial in overweight or obese women reported a circulating adiponectin
      difference, while broader pooled evidence was heterogeneous and did not establish an overall adiponectin
      effect.
    finding_statement: >-
      A 12-week milled-flaxseed trial in overweight or obese women reported a circulating adiponectin
      difference, while broader pooled evidence was heterogeneous and did not establish an overall adiponectin
      effect.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      The tested food was milled whole flaxseed with a balanced diet, not isolated ALA, lignan or fibre. The
      primary report analysed 52 completers after differential dropout and concurrent weight/waist changes.
      Its leptin between-group contrast was not significant. The later synthesis qualifies generalisation and
      is not an independent trial replication.
    synthesis: >-
      The tested food was milled whole flaxseed with a balanced diet, not isolated ALA, lignan or fibre. The
      primary report analysed 52 completers after differential dropout and concurrent weight/waist changes.
      Its leptin between-group contrast was not significant. The later synthesis qualifies generalisation and
      is not an independent trial replication.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Food/form/population-specific physiological response; weight-independent mediation, adipose secretion
      and active constituent are unresolved. No universal hormone target, clinical or brain benefit inferred.
      Small-study effects and pooled uncertainty remain visible.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Ahmadniay Motlagh et al. (2021)
        citation_key: motlagh_flaxseed_adiponectin_2021

          rice for 12 weeks.
        study_finding: >-
          52 completed (29 flax/23 control). Higher adiponectin between groups; leptin contrast not
          significant. Weight and waist also changed.
        directional_finding: >-
          52 completed (29 flax/23 control). Higher adiponectin between groups; leptin contrast not
          significant. Weight and waist also changed.
        assessment:
          study: >-
            60 women aged 25–50 randomised to balanced diet plus 30 g/day milled brown flaxseed or raw milled
            rice for 12 weeks.
          population: >-
            60 women aged 25–50 randomised to balanced diet plus 30 g/day milled brown flaxseed or raw milled
            rice for 12 weeks.
          result: >-
            52 completed (29 flax/23 control). Higher adiponectin between groups; leptin contrast not
            significant. Weight and waist also changed.
          effect_magnitude: >-
            52 completed (29 flax/23 control). Higher adiponectin between groups; leptin contrast not
            significant. Weight and waist also changed.
          evidence_summary: >-
            A defined human food protocol measured an endocrine signal. Admission is limited to studied
            circulating response, not weight-independent mechanism or universal optimisation.
          limitations: >-
            Differential attrition, complete-case analysis and concurrent weight change; no constituent

            No separate supplement identified.

  - id: PM8-F21
    finding_label: Soluble-fibre pooled adipokine evidence does not establish a generic endocrine intervention
    presentation: mechanistic-basis
    fm_rollup: false
    finding_summary: >-
      A soluble-fibre meta-analysis did not establish significant overall changes in circulating leptin or
      adiponectin and reported substantial between-study heterogeneity.
    finding_statement: >-
      A soluble-fibre meta-analysis did not establish significant overall changes in circulating leptin or
      adiponectin and reported substantial between-study heterogeneity.
    finding_interpretation: >-
      The overall estimates include no-effect values; subtype and subgroup findings do not validate a generic
      fibre endocrine claim. This is a secondary synthesis of trials, not a primary experiment or proof that
      every fibre is inactive.
    synthesis: >-
      The overall estimates include no-effect values; subtype and subgroup findings do not validate a generic
      fibre endocrine claim. This is a secondary synthesis of trials, not a primary experiment or proof that
      every fibre is inactive.
    synthesis_limitations: >-
      Heterogeneous preparations, populations and subgroup analyses; blood concentrations do not identify
      local secretion, receptor effects or microbial mediation. Other-BRS benefits do not establish PM8
      engagement.
    synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
    evidence_considered:
      - label: Zeinabi et al. (2023)
        citation_key: zeinabi_soluble_fibre_adipokines_2023

        directional_finding: 'Overall adiponectin SMD −0.49 (95% CI −1.20,0.21), leptin −0.80 (−1.70,0.08); high heterogeneity.'
        assessment:
          study: >-
            Systematic review/dose-response meta-analysis of soluble-fibre supplementation; searches through
            December 2021.
          population: >-
            Systematic review/dose-response meta-analysis of soluble-fibre supplementation; searches through
            December 2021.
          result: 'Overall adiponectin SMD −0.49 (95% CI −1.20,0.21), leptin −0.80 (−1.70,0.08); high heterogeneity.'
          effect_magnitude: 'Overall adiponectin SMD −0.49 (95% CI −1.20,0.21), leptin −0.80 (−1.70,0.08); high heterogeneity.'

system_optimisation_practices:
  - atom_id: PM8-SOP-1
    input: Hypocaloric dietary weight-loss programme in adults with obesity
    input_type: dietary protocol
    biological_role: >-
      A studied dietary programme can alter adipose endocrine output: lower ex vivo leptin release was
      observed in women with obesity; adiponectin responses depend on the study and depot.
    evidence_source: &ref_1
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F16
        - PM8-F13
      citation_keys:
        - you_regional_release_2014
        - arvidsson_hypocaloric_secretion_2004
        - siklova_diet_phase_secretion_2012
        - stomby_diet_glucocorticoid_2015
    evidence_limitation: >-
      Studied 10–20-week programmes and multiphase follow-up predominantly involved women with obesity. Energy
      restriction, weight loss and co-interventions are not independently separable. Ex vivo release is not
      whole-body flux; lower leptin is not a universal goal and adiponectin did not consistently increase. No
      food-specific effect, individual caloric target or brain benefit is inferred.
    dietary_input: true
    reader_description: Context-specific dietary programme with measured local adipokine-release responses.
    description_finding_id: PM8-F16
    optimisation_category: dietary_protocols
    status: admitted
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    protocol:
      population: Adults with obesity; direct release evidence predominantly women.
      tested_exposures:
        - 'Arvidsson: 10 weeks, 600 kcal/day deficit, two macronutrient distributions.'
        - 'You: 20-week hypocaloric programme, with or without aerobic exercise; pooled analysis.'
        - 'Siklova: restriction followed by stabilisation/maintenance; phase-specific endocrine responses.'
        - >-
          Stomby: 24-month dietary weight-loss follow-up in 49 postmenopausal women; decreased adipose
          11β-HSD1 mRNA, not measured activity or local regeneration.
      interpretation: 'Study protocols are retained as evidence context, not a universal effective-dose prescription.'
  - atom_id: PM8-SOP-2
    input: Milled flaxseed in the studied balanced-diet protocol
    input_type: whole-food intervention
    biological_role: The tested milled-flaxseed protocol changed circulating adiponectin in overweight or obese women.
    evidence_source:
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F20
      citation_keys:
        - motlagh_flaxseed_adiponectin_2021
        - abbasi_flaxseed_adipokines_2023
    evidence_limitation: >-
      30 g/day for 12 weeks is the tested protocol, not a universal effective dose. Differential dropout,
      concurrent weight change and pooled null/heterogeneous adiponectin evidence limit generalisation. No
      isolated ALA/lignan/fibre, secretion or brain claim.
    dietary_input: true
    reader_description: Food-specific circulating response; active constituent and generalisability remain unresolved.
    description_finding_id: PM8-F20
    optimisation_category: dietary_protocols
    status: admitted
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    protocol:
      food_id: flax-seeds
      food_href: /docs/foods/flax-seeds

      trial_exposure: 30 g/day for 12 weeks with balanced diet; raw milled rice comparator.
      outcome: Circulating adiponectin; not adipose secretion or receptor responsiveness.
      composition_relationships: Existing ALA food edge is composition evidence only; not an admission of isolated ALA.
lifestyle_priorities:
  - atom_id: PM8-LL-1
    input: Aerobic exercise with context-specific adipose endocrine responses
    input_type: exercise protocol
    biological_role: >-
      Defined aerobic exercise can engage local adipokine regulation, with acute interstitial adiponectin and
      training-related transcript responses in some human contexts.
    evidence_source: &ref_2
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F17
      citation_keys: *ref_0
    evidence_limitation: >-
      Interstitial concentration is not secretion rate; transcripts are not released protein or receptor
      sensitivity. Acute and chronic protocols, sex, adiposity and weight change differ; one training study
      found no tissue-transcript change. No universal dose, optimal hormone direction or brain/clinical
      benefit established.
    dietary_input: false
    reader_description: Qualified local endocrine engagement; variable tissue and circulating responses.
    description_finding_id: PM8-F17
    status: admitted
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    protocol:
      tested_exposures:
        - 'Højbjerre: 1 hour at 55% VO2max; rest-day comparison in 16 men.'
        - 'Moghadasi: 12 weeks, four 45-minute sessions/week at 75–80% VO2max, n=16 randomised men.'
        - >-
          Polak: 12 weeks at 50% VO2max, five sessions/week in 25 women; biopsy subgroup n=8 with null
          transcripts.
      interpretation: 'These are tested exposures, not a recommended optimal dose or proof of equivalent responses.'
