intervention_dominance_assessment:
  evidence_qualification:
    label: Diet-Supported
    rationale: >-
      Dietary weight-reduction programmes demonstrate local adipokine-release engagement in studied obesity
      contexts; aerobic exercise provides a separate qualified route. This does not establish a universally
      beneficial hormone direction or comparative dominance. DHA-specific circulating-signal regulation and a
      bounded milled-flaxseed protocol are now supported; these do not establish principal-route prominence.
    scope_note: >-
      Evidence covers selected adipokine operations, not all receptor or local glucocorticoid functions.
      Energy balance, weight change, sex, depot and protocol remain material.
    routes:
      - group_id: '3.2'
        evidence_basis: intervention-effect
        intervention_effect: >-
          A studied dietary programme can alter adipose endocrine output: lower ex vivo leptin release was
          observed in women with obesity; adiponectin responses depend on the study and depot.
        context: Adults with obesity; direct release evidence predominantly women.
        limitations: >-
          Studied 10–20-week programmes and multiphase follow-up predominantly involved women with obesity.
          Energy restriction, weight loss and co-interventions are not independently separable. Ex vivo
          release is not whole-body flux; lower leptin is not a universal goal and adiponectin did not
          consistently increase. No food-specific effect, individual caloric target or brain benefit is
          inferred.
        evidence_source: *ref_1
      - group_id: '3.3'
        evidence_basis: intervention-effect
        intervention_effect: >-
          Defined aerobic exercise can engage local adipokine regulation, with acute interstitial adiponectin
          and training-related transcript responses in some human contexts.
        context: Small acute and training studies in lean/overweight/obese adults.
        limitations: >-
          Interstitial concentration is not secretion rate; transcripts are not released protein or receptor
          sensitivity. Acute and chronic protocols, sex, adiposity and weight change differ; one training
          study found no tissue-transcript change. No universal dose, optimal hormone direction or
          brain/clinical benefit established.
        evidence_source: *ref_2
      - group_id: '3.1'
        evidence_basis: intervention-effect
        intervention_effect: >-
          Defined oral DHA exposures can modulate circulating adiponectin, an adipose-derived endocrine
          signal.
        context: Selected human trial populations receiving defined DHA formulations.
        limitations: >-
          Admission concerns a circulating hormone response in studied supplement/enriched-oil contexts. It
          does not establish adipose secretion, receptor sensitivity, universal benefit, ordinary-food
          efficacy or a food-effective dose. Mixture and carrier effects are not attributed to DHA alone;
          DHA-specific attribution uses the separate DHA-versus-EPA trial.
        evidence_source: *ref_3
  principal_route_selection:
    disposition: not-established
    selected_groups: []
    rationale: >-
      The complete dietary/lifestyle sweep supports bounded routes, but their coverage and extent do not
      establish one principal mission-wide intervention. Qualification is independent of prominence.
    comparative_limitations: >-
      No universal optimisation target or comparative superiority inferred; local cortisol regeneration and
      receptor responsiveness remain incompletely covered.
    assessments:
      - group_id: '3.2'
        relevance: Directly addresses adipokine release in studied populations.
        directness: Ex vivo adipose release; energy restriction and weight loss inseparable.
        extent: 'Limited populations, depot/phase-specific response; not mission-wide primacy.'
      - group_id: '3.3'
        relevance: Engages local adipokine regulation in selected protocols.
        directness: 'Interstitial concentration and transcripts, not secretion flux.'
        extent: Variable/null responses and small samples preclude principal-route selection.
scientific_findings_intro: >-
  Human tissue, local cortisol and mouse stress-feedback evidence address distinct endocrine operations.
  Intervention measurements do not establish a single adipokine–glucocorticoid–brain causal chain.
sidebar_label: BRS6-4-8 — Adipose Endocrine Regulation
scientific_reconstruction_history:
  - version: 1
    date: '2026-10-26'
    decision: >-
      Approved endocrine mission reconstruction; previous scope preserved without transferring evidential or
      intervention admission status.
    previous_title: Metabolic Inflammation & Adipose Stress Signalling
    previous_mission: >-
      Limit metabolic-inflammatory signalling from adipose stress so whole-body resource allocation is not
      overloaded.
    source_snapshot: >-
      review/brs6-fm4-reconstruction-2026-10-26/before/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm4/brs6-fm4-pm8-metabolic-inflammation-and-adipose-stress-signalling.mdx
    records:
      scientific_findings:
        - id: PM8-F1
          finding_label: Weight loss changes adipose biology in stages
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Moderate weight loss improved insulin action before the measured adipose inflammatory markers
            declined.
          finding_statement: >-
            Moderate weight loss improved insulin action before the measured adipose inflammatory markers
            declined.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            Early metabolic improvement did not require a detectable reduction in the measured subcutaneous
            inflammatory markers; greater losses were accompanied by further tissue changes.
          synthesis: >-
            Early metabolic improvement did not require a detectable reduction in the measured subcutaneous
            inflammatory markers; greater losses were accompanied by further tissue changes.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Later progressive-loss comparisons involved nine participants and were not independently
            randomized; other depots and unmeasured inflammatory processes remain uncertain.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Magkos et al. (2016)
              citation_key: magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: dietary-protocol
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                At 5% loss insulin sensitivity improved without reduced measured systemic or subcutaneous
                inflammation; greater loss altered oxidative-stress, matrix and inflammatory pathways.
              study_exposure: >-
                Weight maintenance versus initial 5% diet-induced loss; later approximately 11% and 16% loss
                with abdominal subcutaneous biopsy and tracer/clamp measurements.
              study_finding: >-
                At 5% loss insulin sensitivity improved without reduced measured systemic or subcutaneous
                inflammation; greater loss altered oxidative-stress, matrix and inflammatory pathways.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  Weight maintenance versus initial 5% diet-induced loss; later approximately 11% and 16% loss
                  with abdominal subcutaneous biopsy and tracer/clamp measurements.
                population: >-
                  40 adults with obesity and insulin resistance randomized; 33 completed the initial
                  comparison and nine continued progressive loss.
                result: >-
                  At 5% loss insulin sensitivity improved without reduced measured systemic or subcutaneous
                  inflammation; greater loss altered oxidative-stress, matrix and inflammatory pathways.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  Actual losses 5.1%, 10.8% and 16.4%; no pooled inflammatory effect or universal threshold
                  estimated.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  At 5% loss insulin sensitivity improved without reduced measured systemic or subcutaneous
                  inflammation; greater loss altered oxidative-stress, matrix and inflammatory pathways.
                limitations: >-
                  Attrition and selected later subgroup; endpoint timing and depot specificity prevent
                  universal mediation claims.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary full text: Methods, Results, Tables 1–4, tissue expression results and Discussion.'
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4833627/'
          connected_supportive_evidence:
            - label: Mohammad & Thiemermann (2021)
              citation_key: mohammad_role_2021
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#mohammad_role_2021
              why_relevant: Reviews a possible gut LPS/TLR4 route into metabolic inflammation.
              why_excluded: >-
                Review evidence and endotoxin assay limitations do not isolate a food-to-adipose intervention;
                the route is contextual.
            - label: Cazzola et al. (2024)
              citation_key: cazzola_magnesium_2024
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#cazzola_magnesium_2024
              why_relevant: Review of magnesium and adipose stress; primary candidate follow-up is assessed in PM8-F8.
              why_excluded: >-
                Not an independent experiment or evidence that extra dietary magnesium reduces human adipose
                inflammation.
            - label: Di Girolamo et al. (2022)
              citation_key: di_girolamo_prevalence_2022
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#di_girolamo_prevalence_2022
              why_relevant: Provides clinical metabolic-comorbidity context rather than an adipose intervention.
              why_excluded: >-
                Does not measure a dietary intervention on adipose stress or establish a PM-to-Phenome causal
                relationship.
            - label: Marcelli et al. (2025)
              citation_key: marcelli_bridging_2025
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#marcelli_bridging_2025
              why_relevant: Provides clinical metabolic-comorbidity context rather than an adipose intervention.
              why_excluded: >-
                Does not measure a dietary intervention on adipose stress or establish a PM-to-Phenome causal
                relationship.
        - id: PM8-F2
          finding_label: Blood-cell cytokines do not identify an adipose source
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: EPA-rich supplementation lowered stimulated immune-cell IL-6 production in medical students.
          finding_statement: EPA-rich supplementation lowered stimulated immune-cell IL-6 production in medical students.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            This source supports an immune-cell effect, while adipose-specific evidence is assessed separately
            in PM8-F6.
          synthesis: >-
            This source supports an immune-cell effect, while adipose-specific evidence is assessed separately
            in PM8-F6.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            No adipose biopsy or adipose-specific endpoint; anxiety results cannot be attributed to adipose
            mediation.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Kiecolt-Glaser et al. (2011)
              citation_key: kiecolt-glaser_omega-3_2011
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#kiecolt-glaser_omega-3_2011
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: supplemental-formulation
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: Stimulated IL-6 production was lower in the supplement group.
              study_exposure: >-
                Double-blind 12-week randomized comparison of 2.5 g/day omega-3 supplement (2085 mg EPA and
                348 mg DHA) versus placebo.
              study_finding: Stimulated IL-6 production was lower in the supplement group.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  Double-blind 12-week randomized comparison of 2.5 g/day omega-3 supplement (2085 mg EPA and
                  348 mg DHA) versus placebo.
                population: 68 medical students.
                result: Stimulated IL-6 production was lower in the supplement group.
                effect_magnitude: 14% lower LPS-stimulated IL-6 production versus controls.
                evidence_summary: Stimulated IL-6 production was lower in the supplement group.
                limitations: A peripheral immune-cell assay cannot locate inflammation in adipose tissue.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary full-text abstract, exposure and outcome definitions.'
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3191260/'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
        - id: PM8-F3
          finding_label: A defined green Mediterranean pattern reduced visceral fat
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            The green Mediterranean diet package reduced MRI-measured visceral fat more than the comparison
            diets in DIRECT-PLUS.
          finding_statement: >-
            The green Mediterranean diet package reduced MRI-measured visceral fat more than the comparison
            diets in DIRECT-PLUS.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            The pattern is a dietary target for visceral adiposity; its individual foods remain candidates for
            component-specific testing. Related circulating-protein changes strengthen relevance to metabolic
            inflammation without proving adipose mediation.
          synthesis: >-
            The pattern is a dietary target for visceral adiposity; its individual foods remain candidates for
            component-specific testing. Related circulating-protein changes strengthen relevance to metabolic
            inflammation without proving adipose mediation.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            One predominantly male workplace cohort; the foods were not independently randomized, fat volume
            is not a biopsy of inflammation, and the protein analysis is not independent replication.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Zelicha et al. (2022)
              citation_key: zelicha_effect_2022
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#zelicha_effect_2022
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: dietary-pattern
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: Green-MED produced greater visceral-fat reduction.
              study_exposure: >-
                18-month healthy dietary guidance, Mediterranean or green-Mediterranean programme, all with
                physical activity; both MED groups received 28 g/day walnuts; green-MED added 3–4 cups/day
                green tea, 100 g/day frozen Mankai and reduced red/processed meat.
              study_finding: Green-MED produced greater visceral-fat reduction.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  18-month healthy dietary guidance, Mediterranean or green-Mediterranean programme, all with
                  physical activity; both MED groups received 28 g/day walnuts; green-MED added 3–4 cups/day
                  green tea, 100 g/day frozen Mankai and reduced red/processed meat.
                population: >-
                  294 randomized adults with abdominal obesity/dyslipidaemia; 88% men; 79.3% had eligible
                  follow-up MRI.
                result: Green-MED produced greater visceral-fat reduction.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  VAT change −14.1% green-MED, −6.0% MED, −4.2% guidance; weight-adjusted green-MED versus MED
                  p=0.042, versus guidance p=0.07.
                evidence_summary: Green-MED produced greater visceral-fat reduction.
                limitations: >-
                  Open-label package, incomplete MRI follow-up and post-randomization adjustment; no isolated
                  food attribution or adipose inflammatory mediation.
                retrieval_scope: >-
                  Primary full text: allocation, exposures, MRI, results and adjusted comparisons; funding
                  includes California Walnuts Commission.
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9523931/'
            - label: Zelicha et al. (2024)
              citation_key: zelicha_proteome_2024
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#zelicha_proteome_2024
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: dietary-pattern
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                MED diets changed inflammation-related circulating proteins; green-MED showed more pronounced
                changes, with protein-cluster changes correlated with VAT reduction.
              study_exposure: Olink circulating-protein panels across the dietary intervention.
              study_finding: >-
                MED diets changed inflammation-related circulating proteins; green-MED showed more pronounced
                changes, with protein-cluster changes correlated with VAT reduction.
              assessment:
                study: Olink circulating-protein panels across the dietary intervention.
                population: 'DIRECT-PLUS participants: the same randomized cohort as the 2022 VAT report.'
                result: >-
                  MED diets changed inflammation-related circulating proteins; green-MED showed more
                  pronounced changes, with protein-cluster changes correlated with VAT reduction.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  Reported adjusted p<0.05 for selected protein changes; no tissue-specific mediated effect
                  quantified.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  MED diets changed inflammation-related circulating proteins; green-MED showed more
                  pronounced changes, with protein-cluster changes correlated with VAT reduction.
                limitations: >-
                  Secondary proteomics, multiplicity and same-cohort dependency; circulating proteins do not
                  establish adipose origin.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38757229/'
          connected_supportive_evidence:
            - label: Willmann et al. (2019)
              citation_key: willmann_red_meat_fibre_2019
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#willmann_red_meat_fibre_2019
              why_relevant: >-
                A separate six-month randomized dietary study compared reduced red meat and increased fibre
                within calorie restriction; visceral fat fell in all groups.
              why_excluded: >-
                No additional cardiometabolic advantage of the food changes was established in that context;
                it does not isolate which green-MED component explains its package result.
            - label: Park et al. (2014)
              citation_key: park_pomegranate_vinegar_2014
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#park_pomegranate_vinegar_2014
              why_relevant: >-
                A different pomegranate-vinegar formulation showed a preliminary CT-measured visceral-fat
                signal in overweight women.
              why_excluded: >-
                Publisher-indexed abstract/result only was recovered; this is a vinegar-plus-extract
                formulation, not pomegranate fruit or a DIRECT-PLUS intervention. Independent efficacy and
                formulation details remain unresolved.
          evidence_dependency:
            - >-
              The 2022 VAT and 2024 proteome publications analyse DIRECT-PLUS (NCT03020186); count one
              intervention cohort, not two replications. PM8-F4 reuses the 2022 study for component
              attribution, not an independent food trial.
        - id: PM8-F4
          finding_label: Walnut-specific visceral-fat effects remain uncertain
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            The green-MED trial identifies walnuts as a food candidate, but does not isolate a walnut-specific
            effect on visceral fat.
          finding_statement: >-
            The green-MED trial identifies walnuts as a food candidate, but does not isolate a walnut-specific
            effect on visceral fat.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            A separate walnut trial was null for visceral fat. A newer walnut-based low-carbohydrate trial
            reported a favourable change interaction, but changed macronutrients as well; component causality
            remains unresolved.
          synthesis: >-
            A separate walnut trial was null for visceral fat. A newer walnut-based low-carbohydrate trial
            reported a favourable change interaction, but changed macronutrients as well; component causality
            remains unresolved.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Different diet packages, fat measurement methods and statistical contrasts cannot establish a
            stand-alone walnut dose or identify ellagitannins as the active ingredient.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Njike et al. (2015)
              citation_key: njike_walnut_body_composition_2015
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#njike_walnut_body_composition_2015
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: whole-food
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: No significant effect on visceral fat or the reported anthropometric measures.
              study_exposure: >-
                Randomized walnut-included (56 g/day) and walnut-excluded dietary periods of six months, with
                or without calorie-adjustment advice.
              study_finding: No significant effect on visceral fat or the reported anthropometric measures.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  Randomized walnut-included (56 g/day) and walnut-excluded dietary periods of six months,
                  with or without calorie-adjustment advice.
                population: 112 adults at risk for diabetes.
                result: No significant effect on visceral fat or the reported anthropometric measures.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  No significant visceral-fat difference; a precise interval for this endpoint was not
                  recovered.
                evidence_summary: No significant effect on visceral fat or the reported anthropometric measures.
                limitations: >-
                  Null result bounds an efficacy claim; it is not proof that every walnut substitution has no
                  effect.
                retrieval_scope: Primary journal abstract/results and body-composition findings.
                source_url: 'https://drc.bmj.com/content/3/1/e000115'
            - label: Dong et al. (2026)
              citation_key: dong_walnut_low_carbohydrate_2026
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#dong_walnut_low_carbohydrate_2026
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: dietary-pattern
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                VFA group-by-time interaction favoured the walnut-based diet; endpoint group comparison was
                nonsignificant.
              study_exposure: >-
                12-week randomized walnut-based low-carbohydrate versus high-carbohydrate low-fat diet; 150
                g/day refers to replaced staple food, not walnut dose.
              study_finding: >-
                VFA group-by-time interaction favoured the walnut-based diet; endpoint group comparison was
                nonsignificant.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week randomized walnut-based low-carbohydrate versus high-carbohydrate low-fat diet; 150
                  g/day refers to replaced staple food, not walnut dose.
                population: >-
                  Adults with central obesity; exact sample and walnut gram dose not independently recovered
                  in this assessment.
                result: >-
                  VFA group-by-time interaction favoured the walnut-based diet; endpoint group comparison was
                  nonsignificant.
                effect_magnitude: VFA interaction p=0.014; no significant between-group VFA difference at week 12.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  VFA group-by-time interaction favoured the walnut-based diet; endpoint group comparison was
                  nonsignificant.
                limitations: >-
                  Abstract-only recovery; combined macronutrient substitution prevents attribution to walnuts
                  alone.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42815962/'
          connected_supportive_evidence:
            - label: Zelicha et al. (2022)
              citation_key: zelicha_effect_2022
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#zelicha_effect_2022
              why_relevant: 'Walnuts were supplied to both MED groups, and intake was associated with VAT loss.'
              why_excluded: >-
                The food association is not an independently randomized walnut effect; the package result is
                assessed in PM8-F3.
          evidence_dependency:
            - >-
              DIRECT-PLUS component associations reuse PM8-F3 evidence; Njike and Dong are separate trials,
              but test different exposures.
        - id: PM8-F5
          finding_label: Tea beverage results depend on formulation
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Some catechin-enriched tea beverages reduced visceral fat, while a newer catechin-plus-inulin
            beverage did not.
          finding_statement: >-
            Some catechin-enriched tea beverages reduced visceral fat, while a newer catechin-plus-inulin
            beverage did not.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            Green tea is a reasonable component to test, with evidence tied to the actual beverage and
            comparator. Ordinary brewed tea, catechin-enriched products and combined formulations are not
            equivalent exposures.
          synthesis: >-
            Green tea is a reasonable component to test, with evidence tied to the actual beverage and
            comparator. Ordinary brewed tea, catechin-enriched products and combined formulations are not
            equivalent exposures.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            The positive and null trials differ in formulation, population and fat assessment; neither
            isolates adipose inflammatory signalling or proves a general brewed-tea effect.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Nagao et al. (2007)
              citation_key: nagao_catechin_adiposity_2007
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#nagao_catechin_adiposity_2007
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: catechin-enriched-beverage
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: The higher-catechin group had greater reductions in visceral and subcutaneous fat area.
              study_exposure: >-
                12-week double-blind comparison of tea beverages with 583 versus 96 mg catechins/day,
                maintaining usual diet/activity.
              study_finding: The higher-catechin group had greater reductions in visceral and subcutaneous fat area.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week double-blind comparison of tea beverages with 583 versus 96 mg catechins/day,
                  maintaining usual diet/activity.
                population: Japanese adults with visceral-fat-type obesity; 240 per-protocol participants.
                result: The higher-catechin group had greater reductions in visceral and subcutaneous fat area.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  Direction significant as reported; precise between-group VAT estimate and interval not
                  recovered.
                evidence_summary: The higher-catechin group had greater reductions in visceral and subcutaneous fat area.
                limitations: >-
                  Per-protocol analysis, enriched beverage and manufacturer affiliation limit transfer to
                  ordinary brewed tea.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17557985/'
            - label: Iino et al. (2026)
              citation_key: iino_catechin_inulin_2026
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#iino_catechin_inulin_2026
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: combination-formulation
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: 'Visceral-fat area did not differ significantly, despite an improvement in HOMA-IR.'
              study_exposure: 12-week double-blind placebo-controlled catechin 400 mg/day plus inulin 2.3 g/day beverage.
              study_finding: 'Visceral-fat area did not differ significantly, despite an improvement in HOMA-IR.'
              assessment:
                study: 12-week double-blind placebo-controlled catechin 400 mg/day plus inulin 2.3 g/day beverage.
                population: 96 randomized Japanese adults with visceral obesity; 91 in primary full-analysis set.
                result: 'Visceral-fat area did not differ significantly, despite an improvement in HOMA-IR.'
                effect_magnitude: VFA change +0.91 versus +4.61 cm²; p=0.243.
                evidence_summary: 'Visceral-fat area did not differ significantly, despite an improvement in HOMA-IR.'
                limitations: >-
                  Combined formulation and bioimpedance VFA; neither positive insulin result nor null fat
                  result isolates tea alone.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41830022/'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
        - id: PM8-F6
          finding_label: Marine omega-3 tissue effects vary with context
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Marine omega-3 formulations can change adipose inflammatory endpoints, but effects differ across
            populations and assays.
          finding_statement: >-
            Marine omega-3 formulations can change adipose inflammatory endpoints, but effects differ across
            populations and assays.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            Biopsy evidence is mixed: Spencer found lower macrophage/MCP-1 endpoints, Hames did not replicate
            a tissue inflammatory benefit despite EPA/DHA incorporation, and Fisk found limited responses in
            obesity. A conditional formulation-specific practice remains provisional rather than a reliable
            general treatment.
          synthesis: >-
            Biopsy evidence is mixed: Spencer found lower macrophage/MCP-1 endpoints, Hames did not replicate
            a tissue inflammatory benefit despite EPA/DHA incorporation, and Fisk found limited responses in
            obesity. A conditional formulation-specific practice remains provisional rather than a reliable
            general treatment.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Doses, baseline metabolic state, depot and assays differ; tissue target engagement does not
            establish improved insulin sensitivity or an ordinary-food equivalent.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Spencer et al. (2013)
              citation_key: spencer_omega3_adipose_2013
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#spencer_omega3_adipose_2013
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: supplemental-formulation
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Adipose macrophages and MCP-1 decreased; capillaries increased; insulin sensitivity did not
                change.
              study_exposure: 12-week placebo-controlled 4 g/day omega-3-acid ethyl ester formulation with adipose biopsies.
              study_finding: >-
                Adipose macrophages and MCP-1 decreased; capillaries increased; insulin sensitivity did not
                change.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week placebo-controlled 4 g/day omega-3-acid ethyl ester formulation with adipose
                  biopsies.
                population: Insulin-resistant adults without diabetes.
                result: >-
                  Adipose macrophages and MCP-1 decreased; capillaries increased; insulin sensitivity did not
                  change.
                effect_magnitude: Reported direction; no harmonized between-trial tissue effect estimate calculated.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Adipose macrophages and MCP-1 decreased; capillaries increased; insulin sensitivity did not
                  change.
                limitations: >-
                  Selected population and high-dose ethyl ester formulation; no isolated EPA versus DHA
                  effect.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23328126/'
            - label: Fisk et al. (2022)
              citation_key: fisk_omega3_adipose_2022
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#fisk_omega3_adipose_2022
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: supplemental-formulation
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Inflammatory transcriptome and oxylipin responses were more limited in participants with
                obesity.
              study_exposure: >-
                12-week double-blind fish-oil concentrate (1.1 g EPA + 0.8 g DHA/day) versus corn oil with
                subcutaneous biopsies.
              study_finding: >-
                Inflammatory transcriptome and oxylipin responses were more limited in participants with
                obesity.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week double-blind fish-oil concentrate (1.1 g EPA + 0.8 g DHA/day) versus corn oil with
                  subcutaneous biopsies.
                population: 45 adults with obesity and 39 normal-weight adults.
                result: >-
                  Inflammatory transcriptome and oxylipin responses were more limited in participants with
                  obesity.
                effect_magnitude: No common pooled estimate; results are endpoint- and population-specific.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Inflammatory transcriptome and oxylipin responses were more limited in participants with
                  obesity.
                limitations: >-
                  Cross-population baseline differences and molecular endpoints prevent a uniform efficacy
                  claim.
                retrieval_scope: >-
                  Indexed primary full-text Summary, Methods and population characteristics; detailed
                  transcript/oxylipin tables not reanalysed.
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8894262/'
            - label: Hames et al. (2017)
              citation_key: hames_omega3_adipose_null_2017
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#hames_omega3_adipose_null_2017
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: supplemental-formulation
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                EPA/DHA incorporation increased, without improved measured adipose inflammation or insulin
                regulation of lipolysis.
              study_exposure: Six-month randomized double-blind omega-3 formulation versus oleate placebo.
              study_finding: No measured tissue benefit despite increased EPA/DHA content.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  Six-month double-blind placebo-controlled trial; 3.9 g/day EPA+DHA (4.2 g total omega-3)
                  versus 4.2 g/day oleate.
                population: >-
                  Insulin-resistant adults with overweight or obesity; 12 supplement and nine placebo
                  completers in the adipose substudy.
                result: >-
                  Adipose EPA/DHA increased, but macrophage content, crown-like structures, senescent cells
                  and insulin regulation of lipolysis did not improve.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  CD68 macrophages 31 to 33 per 100 adipocytes with omega-3 versus 33 to 31 with placebo;
                  interaction p=0.25; lipolysis suppression interaction p=0.11.
                evidence_summary: Tissue delivery was demonstrated without the expected inflammatory or lipolytic benefit.
                limitations: >-
                  Small completer substudy, baseline phenotype differences and subcutaneous-only sampling; not
                  proof of no effect in every metabolic context.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary full-text Methods, Results, Tables 1–2 and Discussion.'
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5445674/'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
        - id: PM8-F7
          finding_label: Adipose stress includes immune and oxidative processes
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Adipose expansion is associated with macrophage inflammatory activity, while acute overnutrition
            can impair metabolism before measured cytokines rise.
          finding_statement: >-
            Adipose expansion is associated with macrophage inflammatory activity, while acute overnutrition
            can impair metabolism before measured cytokines rise.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            Macrophage recruitment and oxidative stress provide complementary biological routes; reducing fat
            mass or a blood cytokine cannot be treated as a complete assay of either route.
          synthesis: >-
            Macrophage recruitment and oxidative stress provide complementary biological routes; reducing fat
            mass or a blood cytokine cannot be treated as a complete assay of either route.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Human macrophage evidence includes observational associations; extreme acute overfeeding in six
            men is not an ordinary-diet experiment or proof of a universal sequence.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Weisberg et al. (2003)
              citation_key: weisberg_adipose_macrophages_2003
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#weisberg_adipose_macrophages_2003
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: tissue-and-animal-mechanistic
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Adipocyte size and adiposity tracked macrophage accumulation; macrophages contributed
                inflammatory gene expression.
              study_exposure: 'Expression profiling, histology and cell-fraction analyses across adiposity.'
              study_finding: >-
                Adipocyte size and adiposity tracked macrophage accumulation; macrophages contributed
                inflammatory gene expression.
              assessment:
                study: 'Expression profiling, histology and cell-fraction analyses across adiposity.'
                population: Mouse obesity models and human subcutaneous adipose samples.
                result: >-
                  Adipocyte size and adiposity tracked macrophage accumulation; macrophages contributed
                  inflammatory gene expression.
                effect_magnitude: Directional histological and molecular evidence; no dietary effect size inferred.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Adipocyte size and adiposity tracked macrophage accumulation; macrophages contributed
                  inflammatory gene expression.
                limitations: >-
                  Mixed species; human associations do not prove macrophage mediation in every obesity
                  phenotype.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/14679176/'
            - label: Boden et al. (2015)
              citation_key: boden_overnutrition_oxidative_2015
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#boden_overnutrition_oxidative_2015
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: experimental-overfeeding
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Oxidative stress and insulin resistance developed without initial increases in the measured
                inflammatory cytokines.
              study_exposure: >-
                Short-term extreme overfeeding at about 2–2.5 times habitual calories with adipose and
                metabolic measures.
              study_finding: >-
                Oxidative stress and insulin resistance developed without initial increases in the measured
                inflammatory cytokines.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  Short-term extreme overfeeding at about 2–2.5 times habitual calories with adipose and
                  metabolic measures.
                population: Six healthy non-obese men.
                result: >-
                  Oxidative stress and insulin resistance developed without initial increases in the measured
                  inflammatory cytokines.
                effect_magnitude: Mechanistic direction retained; no usual-diet dose or food-specific estimate.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Oxidative stress and insulin resistance developed without initial increases in the measured
                  inflammatory cytokines.
                limitations: >-
                  Small acute challenge with major energy excess; oxidative association does not establish
                  every causal step.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5600191/'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
        - id: PM8-F8
          finding_label: Magnesium is a research candidate with context-dependent adipose effects
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            A simple claim that more magnesium reduces adipose stress is not established by the assessed
            evidence.
          finding_statement: >-
            A simple claim that more magnesium reduces adipose stress is not established by the assessed
            evidence.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            The attached review motivates investigation; primary mouse data show that low-magnesium exposure
            can reduce fat accumulation while raising circulating lipids. This challenges a simple
            adiposity-as-benefit interpretation.
          synthesis: >-
            The attached review motivates investigation; primary mouse data show that low-magnesium exposure
            can reduce fat accumulation while raising circulating lipids. This challenges a simple
            adiposity-as-benefit interpretation.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Mouse deficiency is not a human dietary strategy; a PM8-specific human dependency or tissue
            benefit remains unresolved after targeted retrieval.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Kurstjens et al. (2018)
              citation_key: kurstjens_magnesium_adipose_2018
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#kurstjens_magnesium_adipose_2018
              data_level: Animal Data
              exposure_context: dietary-deficiency
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Low magnesium reduced high-fat-induced obesity and altered adipose lipolysis, while blood
                lipids remained high.
              study_exposure: 17-week low- versus normal-magnesium diets crossed with low- or high-fat feeding.
              study_finding: >-
                Low magnesium reduced high-fat-induced obesity and altered adipose lipolysis, while blood
                lipids remained high.
              assessment:
                study: 17-week low- versus normal-magnesium diets crossed with low- or high-fat feeding.
                population: 'Male mice, approximately 12 per diet group, plus a replication experiment.'
                result: >-
                  Low magnesium reduced high-fat-induced obesity and altered adipose lipolysis, while blood
                  lipids remained high.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  High-fat group body weights approximately 47.0 versus 38.6 g, normal versus low magnesium;
                  p<0.05.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Low magnesium reduced high-fat-induced obesity and altered adipose lipolysis, while blood
                  lipids remained high.
                limitations: >-
                  Animal-only severe deficiency, not a human adequacy comparison; smaller fat mass is not
                  proof of healthier adipose signalling.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary full-text Abstract, Methods, Results and lipid/adipose outcomes.'
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6096631/'
          connected_supportive_evidence:
            - label: Cazzola et al. (2024)
              citation_key: cazzola_magnesium_2024
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#cazzola_magnesium_2024
              why_relevant: 'Attached narrative review covers magnesium, oxidative stress and adipose physiology.'
              why_excluded: >-
                Review is a discovery source; its qualitative biology does not establish human PM8 dietary
                efficacy.
        - id: PM8-F9
          finding_label: Exercise can remodel adipose tissue without weight loss
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Exercise training altered subcutaneous adipose structure without weight loss in adults with
            obesity.
          finding_statement: >-
            Exercise training altered subcutaneous adipose structure without weight loss in adults with
            obesity.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            Regular activity is relevant to adipose remodelling, but the observed signalling changes are not a
            blanket reduction in inflammation or proof that one training intensity is superior.
          synthesis: >-
            Regular activity is relevant to adipose remodelling, but the observed signalling changes are not a
            blanket reduction in inflammation or proof that one training intensity is superior.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Counterbalanced allocation between two exercise programmes, no sedentary control and several
            transient responses limit causal and durability claims.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Ahn et al. (2022)
              citation_key: ahn_exercise_adipose_2022
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#ahn_exercise_adipose_2022
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: exercise-training
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Adipocyte size, matrix and capillarization changed; several molecular responses reversed after
                four days.
              study_exposure: >-
                12-week continuous moderate-intensity or interval training, four days/week; weight maintained;
                biopsies one and four days after final session.
              study_finding: >-
                Adipocyte size, matrix and capillarization changed; several molecular responses reversed after
                four days.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week continuous moderate-intensity or interval training, four days/week; weight
                  maintained; biopsies one and four days after final session.
                population: 36 adults with obesity.
                result: >-
                  Adipocyte size, matrix and capillarization changed; several molecular responses reversed
                  after four days.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  No meaningful superiority between training modes reported; no pooled anti-inflammatory
                  effect inferred.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Adipocyte size, matrix and capillarization changed; several molecular responses reversed
                  after four days.
                limitations: >-
                  Allocation was counterbalanced, not described as randomized; transient signalling is not
                  sustained suppression.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary indexed Methods, allocation and Discussion recovered.'
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9058215/'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
        - id: PM8-F10
          finding_label: Sleep restriction can promote visceral-fat accumulation
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Severe sleep restriction increased visceral fat during a controlled experiment with freely
            available food.
          finding_statement: >-
            Severe sleep restriction increased visceral fat during a controlled experiment with freely
            available food.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            Avoiding repeated severe sleep loss is relevant to adipose load. This experiment does not
            establish that sleep extension reverses established adipose inflammation.
          synthesis: >-
            Avoiding repeated severe sleep loss is relevant to adipose load. This experiment does not
            establish that sleep extension reverses established adipose inflammation.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            Small young, predominantly male sample, severe imposed restriction and ad-libitum intake; no
            adipose inflammatory biopsy.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Covassin et al. (2022)
              citation_key: covassin_sleep_visceral_2022
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#covassin_sleep_visceral_2022
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: sleep-restriction
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: Sleep restriction increased energy intake and abdominal visceral-fat accumulation.
              study_exposure: >-
                Randomized inpatient crossover, 14 days of four- versus nine-hour sleep opportunity within
                21-day periods.
              study_finding: Sleep restriction increased energy intake and abdominal visceral-fat accumulation.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  Randomized inpatient crossover, 14 days of four- versus nine-hour sleep opportunity within
                  21-day periods.
                population: '12 healthy non-obese adults, nine men, aged 19–39.'
                result: Sleep restriction increased energy intake and abdominal visceral-fat accumulation.
                effect_magnitude: About 11% VAT accumulation and approximately 308 kcal/day greater intake versus control.
                evidence_summary: Sleep restriction increased energy intake and abdominal visceral-fat accumulation.
                limitations: >-
                  Sleep loss and increased intake act together; not a calorie-matched or sleep-extension
                  reversal trial.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary journal Methods, Discussion and central illustration.'
                source_url: 'https://www.jacc.org/doi/10.1016/j.jacc.2022.01.038'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
        - id: PM8-F11
          finding_label: Time-restricted eating has context-specific adipose findings
          presentation: mechanistic-basis
          fm_rollup: false
          finding_summary: >-
            Time-restricted eating showed exploratory adipose pathway changes in TREAD, but another trial
            found no extra visceral-fat reduction over Mediterranean-diet education.
          finding_statement: >-
            Time-restricted eating showed exploratory adipose pathway changes in TREAD, but another trial
            found no extra visceral-fat reduction over Mediterranean-diet education.
          finding_interpretation: >-
            The 10-hour protocol is a provisional practice for adults with obesity and long baseline eating
            windows; its tissue mechanism remains exploratory.
          synthesis: >-
            The 10-hour protocol is a provisional practice for adults with obesity and long baseline eating
            windows; its tissue mechanism remains exploratory.
          synthesis_limitations: >-
            TREAD selected the greatest weight losers for RNA-seq, and fat endpoints and populations differed
            across trials; timing-independent anti-inflammatory benefit is unproven.
          synthesised_evidence_confidence: not-yet-scored
          evidence_considered:
            - label: Wilkinson et al. (2026)
              citation_key: wilkinson_tread_adipose_2026
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#wilkinson_tread_adipose_2026
              data_level: Human Mechanistic
              exposure_context: dietary-timing-protocol
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: >-
                Weight loss favoured TRE; inflammatory gene-set signals decreased in the selected biopsy
                subgroup.
              study_exposure: >-
                12-week 10-hour personalized eating window plus dietary counselling versus counselling;
                exploratory subcutaneous RNA-seq in seven greatest TRE weight losers and six matched controls.
              study_finding: >-
                Weight loss favoured TRE; inflammatory gene-set signals decreased in the selected biopsy
                subgroup.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week 10-hour personalized eating window plus dietary counselling versus counselling;
                  exploratory subcutaneous RNA-seq in seven greatest TRE weight losers and six matched
                  controls.
                population: >-
                  61 adults with obesity without diabetes; 52 completed; baseline eating window at least 14
                  hours.
                result: >-
                  Weight loss favoured TRE; inflammatory gene-set signals decreased in the selected biopsy
                  subgroup.
                effect_magnitude: >-
                  Weight difference −2.91 kg (95% CI −4.21 to −1.60); no individual transcript significant at
                  FDR<0.05.
                evidence_summary: >-
                  Weight loss favoured TRE; inflammatory gene-set signals decreased in the selected biopsy
                  subgroup.
                limitations: >-
                  Primary endpoint changed after last participant visit but before analysis; selected small
                  tissue subgroup confounds timing with weight-loss response.
                retrieval_scope: 'Primary full text including endpoint amendment, RNA-seq selection, FDR and results.'
                source_url: 'https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13306140/'
            - label: Dote-Montero et al. (2025)
              citation_key: dote_montero_tre_visceral_2025
              href: /docs/papers/BRAIN-Diet-References#dote_montero_tre_visceral_2025
              data_level: Human Study
              exposure_context: dietary-timing-protocol
              evidence_source: bounded-external-search
              directional_finding: Adding TRE did not confer significant additional VAT reduction.
              study_exposure: >-
                12-week randomized early, late or self-selected eight-hour eating window plus
                Mediterranean-diet education versus education alone.
              study_finding: Adding TRE did not confer significant additional VAT reduction.
              assessment:
                study: >-
                  12-week randomized early, late or self-selected eight-hour eating window plus
                  Mediterranean-diet education versus education alone.
                population: 197 adults with overweight or obesity.
                result: Adding TRE did not confer significant additional VAT reduction.
                effect_magnitude: No extra VAT benefit reported; precise contrasts not recovered.
                evidence_summary: Adding TRE did not confer significant additional VAT reduction.
                limitations: >-
                  Different baseline windows and protocol; a null VAT endpoint does not directly refute a
                  tissue transcript result.
                retrieval_scope: Primary abstract and indexed study results; full methods not independently recovered.
                source_url: 'https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39775037/'
          connected_supportive_evidence: []
      dietary_input_traceability:
        - atom_id: PM8-DIT-1
          input: Green Mediterranean dietary pattern
          input_type: dietary pattern
          biological_role: >-
            Reduces visceral adipose volume in the tested weight-loss programme, with associated changes in
            circulating inflammation-related proteins.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F3
            citation_keys:
              - zelicha_effect_2022
              - zelicha_proteome_2024
          evidence_limitation: >-
            The randomized evidence applies to the combined pattern with physical activity; individual food
            effects and adipose inflammatory mediation remain uncertain.
          dietary_input: true
          dietary_input_note: >-
            Dietary state-regulation relationship at pattern level; not nutritional indispensability or an
            independently effective dose for any component.
      dietary_lever_atoms:
        - atom_id: PM8-DIT-1
          requirement_classification: direct
          relationship_mode: state-regulation
          relationship_layer: dietary-requirement
          dietary_addressability: direct
          claim_ceiling: modulation-demonstrated
      dietary_lever_presentations:
        - atom_id: PM8-DIT-1
          label: Green Mediterranean dietary pattern
          presentation_section: 3.1.1
          reader_description: >-
            This tested pattern combined walnuts (28 g/day), green tea (3–4 cups/day), Mankai (100 g/day
            frozen plant in a shake), and reduced red/processed meat. It reduced visceral fat more than the
            comparison diets in adults with abdominal obesity. These foods are targets for further testing:
            their individual contributions remain uncertain, walnut evidence is mixed, and enriched tea
            beverages are not interchangeable with ordinary brewed tea.
          description_finding_id: PM8-F3
      system_optimisation_practices:
        - atom_id: PM8-SOP-1
          food_strategy_scope: dietary-protocol
          food_strategy_id: food-strategy-4601009eb275
          input: Diet-induced weight reduction when excess adiposity is relevant
          input_type: context-specific dietary protocol
          biological_role: >-
            Progressive diet-induced loss altered subcutaneous adipose tissue pathways; moderate initial loss
            improved insulin action before measured inflammation declined.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F1
            citation_keys:
              - magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
          evidence_limitation: >-
            Later progressive-loss comparisons involved nine participants and were not independently
            randomized; other depots and unmeasured inflammatory processes remain uncertain.
          optimisation_category: dietary_protocols
          reader_description: >-
            A diet-induced weight-reduction programme changed adipose tissue biology in adults with obesity.
            Early insulin improvements preceded the measured inflammatory decline, and later tissue
            comparisons were small.
          description_finding_id: PM8-F1
          dietary_input: true
          dietary_input_note: Dietary energy-reduction programme; not a universal weight-loss prescription.
        - atom_id: PM8-SOP-2
          input: Marine omega-3 formulations in selected metabolic contexts
          input_type: conditional supplementation
          biological_role: >-
            Tested marine omega-3 formulations altered adipose macrophage, chemokine, transcript or oxylipin
            endpoints in selected adults, with variable responses.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F6
            citation_keys:
              - spencer_omega3_adipose_2013
              - fisk_omega3_adipose_2022
              - hames_omega3_adipose_null_2017
          evidence_limitation: >-
            Doses, baseline metabolic state, depot and assays differ; tissue target engagement does not
            establish improved insulin sensitivity or an ordinary-food equivalent.
          dietary_input: true
          dietary_input_note: Supplement exposure only; no ordinary-food dose or walnut-ALA equivalence established.
          optimisation_category: conditional_supplementation
          reader_description: >-
            Adipose biopsy trials have mixed results: some tissue endpoints improved, while another trial
            found no inflammatory benefit despite EPA/DHA incorporation. This is a provisional
            formulation-specific option for further testing, without an established ordinary-food equivalent.
          description_finding_id: PM8-F6
          food_strategy_scope: supplementation
          food_strategy_id: food-strategy-brs6-pm8-marine-omega3
        - atom_id: PM8-SOP-3
          input: Ten-hour eating window for adults with obesity and long baseline eating windows
          input_type: dietary timing protocol
          biological_role: >-
            The TREAD protocol increased weight and fat loss, with exploratory subcutaneous inflammatory
            pathway changes in a selected biopsy subgroup.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F11
            citation_keys:
              - wilkinson_tread_adipose_2026
              - dote_montero_tre_visceral_2025
          evidence_limitation: >-
            TREAD selected the greatest weight losers for RNA-seq, and fat endpoints and populations differed
            across trials; timing-independent anti-inflammatory benefit is unproven.
          dietary_input: true
          dietary_input_note: >-
            Defined eating-window exposure; not an independently proven timing effect or universal fasting
            requirement.
          optimisation_category: dietary_protocols
          reader_description: >-
            A small trial supports this exact protocol, while the adipose analysis was exploratory and
            selected the greatest weight losers. A separate trial found no extra visceral-fat benefit from
            adding time restriction to Mediterranean-diet education.
          description_finding_id: PM8-F11
          food_strategy_scope: mixed-food-and-timing
          food_strategy_id: food-strategy-brs6-pm8-tread-10h
      lifestyle_priorities:
        - atom_id: PM8-LS-1
          input: Maintain regular physical activity
          input_type: physical activity
          biological_role: >-
            Exercise training changed adipocyte size, extracellular matrix and capillarization without weight
            loss in the assessed study.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F9
            citation_keys:
              - ahn_exercise_adipose_2022
          evidence_limitation: >-
            Counterbalanced allocation between two exercise programmes, no sedentary control and several
            transient responses limit causal and durability claims.
          dietary_input: false
          reader_description: >-
            Regular training can alter adipose structure even without weight loss. The assessed comparison
            does not establish a general sustained anti-inflammatory effect or a superior training intensity.
          description_finding_id: PM8-F9
        - atom_id: PM8-LS-2
          input: Protect adequate sleep opportunity
          input_type: sleep behaviour
          biological_role: >-
            Avoids the severe sleep-restriction exposure that increased energy intake and visceral-fat
            accumulation in the crossover experiment.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F10
            citation_keys:
              - covassin_sleep_visceral_2022
          evidence_limitation: >-
            Small young, predominantly male sample, severe imposed restriction and ad-libitum intake; no
            adipose inflammatory biopsy.
          dietary_input: false
          reader_description: >-
            A controlled sleep-loss experiment increased visceral fat. Evidence for reversing established
            adipose inflammation by sleep extension remains a separate question.
          description_finding_id: PM8-F10
      pending_actions:
        - input: 'Walnuts, green tea, Mankai and red/processed-meat substitution'
          action: >-
            Component-specific adipose testing; retain mixed/null evidence and distinguish package effects
            from individual food efficacy.
          finding_ids:

            - PM8-F4
            - PM8-F5
        - input: Pomegranate and urolithin A
          action: >-
            Separate ordinary pomegranate, the pomegranate-vinegar formulation and urolithin supplementation.

        - input: Magnesium

      kc_applicability_adjudications:
        - kc_id: BRS6(KC1)
          ikc_id: BRS6(KC1)
          arm_id: PM8-KC1-review
          disposition: unresolved
          proposition: >-
            Does the narrowed circulating-glucose availability pool establish a PM-specific upstream supply or
            conditional constraint?
          rationale: >-
            The existing KC1 assessment narrows the pool to circulating glucose availability. Its
            cerebral-energy relevance does not automatically admit this PM or a checklist of meal nutrients.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F1
            citation_keys:
              - magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
          evidence_limitation: >-
            Source-supported availability-to-adipose-stress pathway, separate from excess energy intervention
            or systemic cytokines.
          remaining_gap: >-
            Source-supported availability-to-adipose-stress pathway, separate from excess energy intervention
            or systemic cytokines.
          review_record: system/brs6-kc1-evidence-review.md
        - kc_id: BRS6(KC2)
          ikc_id: BRS6(KC2)
          arm_id: PM8-KC2-review
          disposition: unresolved
          proposition: >-
            Does the current stress-response micronutrient/lipid checklist define a coherent shared resource
            relationship?
          rationale: >-
            The existing KC2 evidence review finds no coherent shared pool in the current checklist.
            Individual magnesium, fatty-acid or other decisions remain independent; this does not exclude
            their supported PM biology.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F1
            citation_keys:
              - magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
          evidence_limitation: >-
            The current pool definition does not establish a coherent relationship. This is an unresolved
            definition/applicability gap, not evidence that every constituent fails to constrain this PM.
          remaining_gap: >-
            A canonical coherent pool definition and separately vetted membership would be needed before
            reconsidering pool admission.
          review_record: system/brs6-kc2-evidence-review.md

            '3.2':
              - PM8-SOP-1
              - PM8-SOP-2
              - PM8-SOP-3
          compared_biological_jobs:
            - The assessed programme changed measured adipose tissue pathways with progressive loss.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F1
            citation_keys:
              - magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
          gap: >-
            Source-supported availability-to-adipose-stress pathway, separate from excess energy intervention
            or systemic cytokines.
          rationale: >-
            No additional public KC disclosure is admitted. Scientific applicability and public distinctness
            are separate; review the exact gap, not name/type/citation differences. The new dietary pattern
            and protocols do not establish an upstream glucose pool or a coherent shared micronutrient
            constraint.
        - relationship_id: PM8-KC2-CANDIDATE
          kc_id: BRS6(KC2)
          disposition: unresolved
          biological_job: >-
            Does the current stress-response micronutrient/lipid checklist define a coherent shared resource
            relationship?
          compared_records:
            3.1.1:
              - PM8-DIT-1
            3.1.2: []
            '3.2':
              - PM8-SOP-1
              - PM8-SOP-2
              - PM8-SOP-3
          compared_biological_jobs:
            - The assessed programme changed measured adipose tissue pathways with progressive loss.
          evidence_source:
            finding_ids:
              - PM8-F1
            citation_keys:
              - magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
          gap: >-
            A canonical coherent pool definition and separately vetted membership would be needed before
            reconsidering pool admission.
          rationale: >-
            No additional public KC disclosure is admitted. Scientific applicability and public distinctness
            are separate; review the exact gap, not name/type/citation differences. The new dietary pattern
            and protocols do not establish an upstream glucose pool or a coherent shared micronutrient
            constraint.
      intervention_dominance_assessment:
        evidence_qualification:
          label: Diet-Supported
          rationale: >-
            Dietary pattern and energy-reduction studies engage adipose volume or biopsy endpoints in selected
            populations; no universal efficacy ranking follows.
          routes:
            - group_id: '3.1'
              evidence_basis: intervention-effect
              intervention_effect: >-
                The green Mediterranean diet package reduced MRI-measured visceral fat more than the
                comparison diets in DIRECT-PLUS.
              context: Adults with abdominal obesity; combined diet and physical-activity programme.
              limitations: >-
                One predominantly male workplace cohort; the foods were not independently randomized, fat
                volume is not a biopsy of inflammation, and the protein analysis is not independent
                replication.
              evidence_source:
                finding_ids:
                  - PM8-F3
                citation_keys:
                  - zelicha_effect_2022
                  - zelicha_proteome_2024
            - group_id: '3.2'
              evidence_basis: intervention-effect
              intervention_effect: Defined dietary and supplemental protocols changed tissue or fat endpoints.
              context: Protocol-specific selected populations.
              limitations: Tissue responses vary; no independent component or universal protocol benefit.
              evidence_source:
                finding_ids:
                  - PM8-F1
                  - PM8-F6
                  - PM8-F11
                citation_keys:
                  - magkos_weight_loss_adipose_2016
                  - spencer_omega3_adipose_2013
                  - fisk_omega3_adipose_2022
                  - wilkinson_tread_adipose_2026
            - group_id: '3.3'
              evidence_basis: intervention-effect
              intervention_effect: Exercise altered adipose structure; imposed sleep loss increased visceral fat.
              context: Training study and small inpatient sleep-loss crossover.
              limitations: Different endpoints and designs; no comparative superiority inferred.
              evidence_source:
                finding_ids:
                  - PM8-F9
                  - PM8-F10
                citation_keys:
                  - ahn_exercise_adipose_2022
                  - covassin_sleep_visceral_2022
        principal_route_selection:
          disposition: established
          selected_groups:
            - '3.1'
            - '3.2'
          rationale: >-
            Dietary pattern evidence directly addresses visceral adiposity, while defined weight-reduction and
            other protocols provide complementary tissue-specific evidence.
          joint_prominence_rationale: >-
            Pattern selection and defined protocols answer distinct practical questions; showing both makes
            the food targets and direct tissue evidence accessible without treating them as equally effective.
          comparative_limitations: >-
            No head-to-head comparison establishes superiority across foods, supplements, protocols and
            lifestyle routes; this is relevance-based prominence.
          assessments:
            - group_id: '3.1'
              relevance: The pattern reduces the visceral adipose load relevant to the PM.
              directness: >-
                MRI fat volume and circulating proteins are measured; adipose inflammatory mediation remains
                unproven.
              extent: 'One 18-month cohort, predominantly men, with package-level attribution.'
            - group_id: '3.2'
              relevance: Defined practices include direct adipose biopsy endpoints.
              directness: Weight-reduction and omega-3 studies measure tissue; TREAD biopsy evidence is exploratory.
              extent: 'Selected populations and protocols, with small or variable tissue results.'
      intervention_dominance: Diet-Supported
      intervention_breakdown: Food-State Leaning
      phenome_relationships: []
scientific_principles:
  - id: PM8-SP1
    provenance_kind: curated-biochemical
    title: 11β-HSD1 cortisone–cortisol interconversion
    proposition: >-
      HSD11B1 catalyses NADP(H)-dependent cortisone–cortisol interconversion and predominantly acts as a
      reductase in vivo.
    mechanism: >-
      Local glucocorticoid regeneration; reaction participation of NADPH is distinct from a dietary
      requirement.
    scope_limitations: >-
      Molecular reaction identity does not establish its net rate in every tissue, dietary niacin limitation,
      or an optimal cortisol concentration.
    classification_rationale: Curated enzyme/reaction identity; context-dependent human regulation is assessed separately in SF13.
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    sources:
      - label: UniProtKB P28845 — HSD11B1
        database: UniProt
        accession: P28845
        href: 'https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P28845/entry'
        version: 231; annotation 2026-09-02
        accessed: '2026-10-26'
  - id: PM8-SP2
    provenance_kind: curated-biochemical
    title: Leptin and adiponectin are distinct endocrine molecules
    proposition: >-
      Leptin and adiponectin are distinct adipokines with receptor identities LEPR and ADIPOR1/ADIPOR2,
      respectively.
    mechanism: >-
      Adipose endocrine communication; production, release, circulating exposure and target responsiveness are
      separate relationships.
    scope_limitations: >-
      No claim that concentrations measure secretion or receptor sensitivity; no dietary or clinical effect
      follows from molecular identity.
    classification_rationale: 'Curated molecular and receptor relationships, not an intervention synthesis.'
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    sources:
      - label: UniProtKB P41159 — Leptin
        database: UniProt
        accession: P41159
        href: 'https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P41159/entry'
        version: 221; annotation 2026-09-02
        accessed: '2026-10-26'
      - label: UniProtKB Q15848 — Adiponectin
        database: UniProt
        accession: Q15848
        href: 'https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q15848/entry'
        version: 233; annotation 2026-09-02
        accessed: '2026-10-26'
