  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-energy-restriction
    title: Dietary energy restriction and weight-loss maintenance
    status: admitted
    question: >-
      Does this candidate engage the approved PM8 endocrine operation at its claimed exposure and evidential
      level?
    section: '3.2'
    relationship_assessment:
      type_a: Not a biochemical Capacity input.
      type_b: >-
        Independent intervention admission: measured PM-specific local response within defined
        protocol/context.
      delivery: Direct relative to measured local adipose response; no intermediary dietary precursor is asserted.
    reason: >-
      Supports bounded dietary-programme SOP, not universal hormone optimisation. Release subgroup n=27,
      expression n=42. No untreated comparator for pooled analysis; exercise could not be isolated.
      Whole-tissue ex vivo release is not in vivo flux. Population, depot, weight loss and energy balance
      constrain interpretation. Independent qualified support for dietary programme; retain null adiponectin
      result. No weight-stable comparator; cannot separate energy deficit from weight loss. Adiponectin null
      qualifies You abdominal response; no macronutrient superiority. Tested deficit is a study exposure, not
      an effective-dose prescription. Phase qualification of admitted dietary programme. Uncontrolled
      multiphase programme; cellular source and energy-balance phase matter. Does not justify sustained
      maximisation of leptin suppression or a general very-low-calorie diet recommendation.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      Broader populations, weight-independent mediation and secretion/flux or receptor outcomes would extend
      the current bounded admission.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F16
    evidence_source:
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F16
      citation_keys:
        - you_regional_release_2014
        - arvidsson_hypocaloric_secretion_2004
        - siklova_diet_phase_secretion_2012
    source_assessments:
      - citation_key: you_regional_release_2014
        doi: 10.1002/oby.20743
        title: >-
          Regional adipose tissue hormone/cytokine production before and after weight loss in abdominally
          obese women.
        year: '2014'
        authors:
          - You T
          - Wang X
          - Murphy KM
          - Lyles MF
          - Demons JL
          - Yang R
          - Gong DW
          - Nicklas BJ
        label: You et al. (2014)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          42 older abdominally obese women; 20-week hypocaloric programme, with or without aerobic exercise;
          pooled pre/post analysis. Ex vivo release measured in 27 women.
        endpoint: >-
          Abdominal/gluteal tissue leptin and adiponectin gene expression and ex vivo release; plasma
          separately.
        result: >-
          Weight loss 10.1 ± 0.7 kg. Leptin release decreased in both depots; adiponectin release increased
          only abdominally. Tissue and circulating changes were not significantly correlated.
        limitations: >-
          Release subgroup n=27, expression n=42. No untreated comparator for pooled analysis; exercise could
          not be isolated. Whole-tissue ex vivo release is not in vivo flux. Population, depot, weight loss
          and energy balance constrain interpretation.
        decision: 'Supports bounded dietary-programme SOP, not universal hormone optimisation.'
        access: >-
          Full primary Methods/Results/Discussion and figures recovered through Scite; all 21,177 body
          characters reviewed.
        finding_id: PM8-F16
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1002/oby.20743'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 39
      - &ref_4
        citation_key: arvidsson_hypocaloric_secretion_2004
        doi: 10.2337/diabetes.53.8.1966
        title: Effects of different hypocaloric diets on protein secretion from adipose tissue of obese women.
        year: '2004'
        authors:
          - Arvidsson E
          - Viguerie N
          - Andersson I
          - Verdich C
          - Langin D
          - Arner P
        label: Arvidsson et al. (2004)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          40 women with obesity randomised to 10-week hypocaloric low-fat/high-carbohydrate or
          moderate-fat/moderate-carbohydrate diets; deficit 600 kcal/day.
        endpoint: >-
          Subcutaneous adipose ex vivo protein secretion and transcripts, with circulating concentrations
          separately.
        result: >-
          Approximately 7.5% weight loss and 40% lower leptin secretion; adiponectin secretion unchanged. Diet
          arms had similar responses.
        limitations: >-
          No weight-stable comparator; cannot separate energy deficit from weight loss. Adiponectin null
          qualifies You abdominal response; no macronutrient superiority. Tested deficit is a study exposure,
          not an effective-dose prescription.
        decision: Independent qualified support for dietary programme; retain null adiponectin result.
        access: Primary abstract and accessible journal/PDF excerpts; complete bias appraisal remains limited.
        finding_id: PM8-F16
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.2337/diabetes.53.8.1966'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 40
      - citation_key: siklova_diet_phase_secretion_2012
        doi: 10.1210/jc.2011-2380
        title: >-
          Adipose tissue secretion and expression of adipocyte-produced and stromavascular fraction-produced
          adipokines vary during multiple phases of weight-reducing dietary intervention in obese women.
        year: '2012'
        authors:
          - Siklova-Vitkova M
          - Klimcakova E
          - Polak J
          - Kovacova Z
          - Tencerova M
          - Rossmeislova L
          - Bajzova M
          - Langin D
          - Stich V
        label: Siklova-Vitkova et al. (2012)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          48 women with obesity: 1-month very-low-calorie diet then 5 months stabilisation/maintenance;
          release subgroup 26, transcript subgroup 22.
        endpoint: 'Subcutaneous adipose explant secretion, transcripts and plasma during different programme phases.'
        result: >-
          Adipocyte-produced leptin measures fell during very-low-calorie feeding and rose during
          stabilisation; stromavascular adipokines followed different patterns.
        limitations: >-
          Uncontrolled multiphase programme; cellular source and energy-balance phase matter. Does not justify
          sustained maximisation of leptin suppression or a general very-low-calorie diet recommendation.
        decision: Phase qualification of admitted dietary programme.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: PM8-F16
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1210/jc.2011-2380'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 41
    admitted_atom_id: PM8-SOP-1
    show_in_gap_summary: false
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    history:
      - version: 1
        date: '2026-10-26'
        decision: >-
          admitted in full mission-specific rerun; no automatic ownership transfer or dietary-food
          relationship.

        authors:

          - Landt M
          - Goodrick SJ
          - Mohamed-Ali V
          - Coppack SW

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-macronutrients
    title: 'Protein, carbohydrate and fat composition'
    status: under-investigation
    question: >-
      Does this candidate engage the approved PM8 endocrine operation at its claimed exposure and evidential
      level?
    section: '3.2'
    relationship_assessment:
      type_a: Not a biochemical Capacity input.
      type_b: No protocol-specific PM8 admission established.
      delivery: Not assigned to an unadmitted intervention.
    reason: >-
      No protein input admitted; preserve distinction between satiety, circulating leptin and receptor
      sensitivity. Sequential design; central leptin sensitivity inferred, not measured. No direct adipose
      secretion/receptor assay or protein-specific PM8 admission. Meal-related local engagement established
      narrowly; defer an actionable food/composition recommendation. Different small cohorts, non-isolated
      carbohydrate, oral versus intravenous exposure. Concentration difference is not equivalent to fully
      quantified secretion flux. No optimal meal or carbohydrate requirement established. Independent
      qualified support for dietary programme; retain null adiponectin result. No weight-stable comparator;
      cannot separate energy deficit from weight loss. Adiponectin null qualifies You abdominal response; no
      macronutrient superiority. Tested deficit is a study exposure, not an effective-dose prescription.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      A defined exposure with appropriate PM8 endocrine measurements and supported delivery/attribution,
      preserving confounders and null evidence.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F16
    evidence_source:
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F16
      citation_keys:
        - weigle_protein_leptin_2005
        - evans_meal_leptin_2001
        - arvidsson_hypocaloric_secretion_2004
    source_assessments:
      - citation_key: weigle_protein_leptin_2005
        doi: 10.1093/ajcn.82.1.41
        title: >-
          A high-protein diet induces sustained reductions in appetite, ad libitum caloric intake, and body
          weight despite compensatory changes in diurnal plasma leptin and ghrelin concentrations.
        year: '2005'
        authors:
          - Weigle DS
          - Breen PA
          - Matthys CC
          - Callahan HS
          - Meeuws KE
          - Burden VR
          - Purnell JQ
        label: Weigle et al. (2005)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: '19 adults: sequential 15% protein, isocaloric 30% protein, then ad-libitum 30% protein diet.'
        endpoint: '24-hour circulating leptin and satiety, energy intake and weight.'
        result: >-
          Satiety increased on isocaloric higher protein without changed leptin AUC; ad-libitum phase reduced
          intake and weight with lower leptin.
        limitations: >-
          Sequential design; central leptin sensitivity inferred, not measured. No direct adipose
          secretion/receptor assay or protein-specific PM8 admission.
        decision: >-
          No protein input admitted; preserve distinction between satiety, circulating leptin and receptor
          sensitivity.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: null
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1093/ajcn.82.1.41'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 50
      - citation_key: evans_meal_leptin_2001
        doi: 10.1042/cs1000493
        title: >-
          Carbohydrate and fat have different effects on plasma leptin concentrations and adipose tissue
          leptin production.
        year: '2001'
        authors:
          - Evans K
          - Clark ML
          - Frayn KN
        label: Evans et al. (2001)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          6 volunteers: oral 40 g fat/9.6 g carbohydrate versus intravenous fat; separate 8 volunteers: mixed
          meal 37 g fat/100 g carbohydrate.
        endpoint: Paired arterialised/adipose venous leptin concentration differences over 6 hours.
        result: >-
          Leptin venous–arterial difference fell after fat loads and rose after the mixed meal, peaking at 240
          minutes.
        limitations: >-
          Different small cohorts, non-isolated carbohydrate, oral versus intravenous exposure. Concentration
          difference is not equivalent to fully quantified secretion flux. No optimal meal or carbohydrate
          requirement established.
        decision: >-
          Meal-related local engagement established narrowly; defer an actionable food/composition
          recommendation.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: PM8-F16
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1042/cs1000493'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 49

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-epa-dha
    title: EPA and DHA
    status: under-investigation
    question: >-
      Does this candidate engage the approved PM8 endocrine operation at its claimed exposure and evidential
      level?
    section: 3.1.1
    relationship_assessment:
      type_a: No complete PM-specific functional dependency and dietary supply claim established in this rerun.
      type_b: Relevant signals retained; essential delivery or endocrine-engagement link not fully established.
      delivery: Direct/Derived not assigned to an unadmitted relationship.
    reason: >-
      Earlier blanket essential-delivery wording is superseded: oral fish oil with tissue incorporation
      demonstrates mixture delivery. DHA-specific circulating regulation is now independently admitted from
      ComparED. No isolated EPA or local secretion relationship is inferred.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      A defined exposure with appropriate PM8 endocrine measurements and supported delivery/attribution,
      preserving confounders and null evidence.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F18
    evidence_source:
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F18
      citation_keys: *ref_5
    source_assessments:
      - citation_key: tishinsky_epa_adiponectin_2011
        doi: 10.1038/oby.2010.186
        title: >-
          Eicosapentaenoic acid and rosiglitazone increase adiponectin in an additive and PPARγ-dependent
          manner in human adipocytes.
        year: '2011'
        authors:
          - Tishinsky JM
          - Ma DW
          - Robinson LE
        label: Tishinsky et al. (2011)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          Isolated cultured human adipocytes exposed for 48 hours to 100 µM free EPA or DHA, with PPARγ
          perturbation and rosiglitazone comparisons.
        endpoint: 'Secreted adiponectin, intracellular protein and PPARγ-dependent response.'
        result: Adiponectin secretion rose 88% with EPA and 47% with DHA; antagonist responses differed.
        limitations: >-
          Cultured free-fatty-acid exposure is not dietary delivery. Rosiglitazone is pharmacological; no
          ordinary-fish, food-dose or clinical equivalence.
        decision: Mechanistic Finding; nutrient admission deferred pending essential exposure/engagement bridge.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: PM8-F18
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1038/oby.2010.186'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 46
      - citation_key: wu_fish_oil_adiponectin_2013
        doi: 10.1210/jc.2012-3899
        title: >-
          Effect of fish oil on circulating adiponectin: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized
          controlled trials.
        year: '2013'
        authors:
          - Wu JH
          - Cahill LE
          - Mozaffarian D
        label: Wu et al. (2013)
        publication_class: secondary
        exposure: >-
          Systematic review/meta-analysis: 14 fish-oil trial arms, 682 intervention/641 control; two
          fish-feeding arms separately.
        endpoint: 'Circulating adiponectin, not adipose secretion.'
        result: >-
          Fish-oil pooled +0.37 µg/mL (95% CI 0.07–0.67), substantial heterogeneity; publication-bias
          adjustment +0.18 (−0.15 to 0.52). Fish-feeding estimate −0.01 (−0.65 to 0.64).
        limitations: >-
          Secondary synthesis; do not double-count included trials. Blood concentration does not identify
          secretion mechanism; heterogeneous formulations and publication bias limit coherence.
        decision: >-
          Supports physiological dietary response but does not close local secretory mediation or
          ordinary-fish equivalence.
        access: Full accessible review text and abstract; synthesis not an independent primary experiment.
        finding_id: PM8-F18
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1210/jc.2012-3899'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-meal-time
    title: Meal timing and time-restricted eating
    status: under-investigation
    question: >-
      Does this candidate engage the approved PM8 endocrine operation at its claimed exposure and evidential
      level?
    section: '3.2'
    relationship_assessment:
      type_a: Not a biochemical Capacity input.
      type_b: No protocol-specific PM8 admission established.
      delivery: Not assigned to an unadmitted intervention.
    reason: >-
      Physiological timing evidence retained; no PM8-specific optimisation protocol admitted. Does not measure
      adipose leptin secretion or receptor responsiveness. Acute timing/energy-expenditure interactions;
      direction of leptin change is not an endocrine-benefit criterion. Timing candidate deferred for PM8; no
      transfer to another PM without its own admission. Ghrelin/appetite are not adipose adipokine secretion
      or local glucocorticoid responsiveness. Small, short study. Circadian source lead; not PM8 intervention
      engagement. Adipose tissue measurement alone does not establish adipokine output, receptor
      responsiveness or local cortisol regeneration.

      A defined exposure with appropriate PM8 endocrine measurements and supported delivery/attribution,
      preserving confounders and null evidence.

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-aerobic
    title: Aerobic exercise
    status: admitted
    question: >-
      Does this candidate engage the approved PM8 endocrine operation at its claimed exposure and evidential
      level?
    section: '3.3'
    relationship_assessment:
      type_a: Not a biochemical Capacity input.
      type_b: >-
        Independent intervention admission: measured PM-specific local response within defined
        protocol/context.
      delivery: Direct relative to measured local adipose response; no intermediary dietary precursor is asserted.
    reason: >-
      Supports bounded local exercise engagement, not a universal adiponectin-raising protocol. Interstitial
      concentration is not secretion rate; blood flow, recovery and compartment distribution matter. Acute
      small male sample does not define chronic response, receptor sensitivity or clinical benefit. Qualified
      training support; no generic secretion claim. Transcription is not secretion or receptor responsiveness.
      Small sample; weight-independent mediation and optimal exercise dose not established. Retain
      contradictory/contextual evidence; limits exercise generalisation. Small biopsy subgroup; uncontrolled
      pre/post design. Important contextual null, not direct refutation of a different acute interstitial
      measurement.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      Broader populations, weight-independent mediation and secretion/flux or receptor outcomes would extend
      the current bounded admission.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F17
    evidence_source:
      finding_ids:
        - PM8-F17
      citation_keys: *ref_0
    source_assessments:
      - citation_key: hojbjerre_exercise_adiponectin_2007
        doi: 10.1530/EJE-07-0213
        title: >-
          Acute exercise increases adipose tissue interstitial adiponectin concentration in healthy overweight
          and lean subjects.
        year: '2007'
        authors:
          - Højbjerre L
          - Rosenzweig M
          - Dela F
          - Bruun JM
          - Stallknecht B
        label: Højbjerre et al. (2007)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          8 overweight and 8 lean men; rest comparison day versus 1 hour exercise at 55% VO2max with pre/post
          subcutaneous biopsy.
        endpoint: >-
          Local interstitial adiponectin by large-pore microdialysis; adipose transcripts and plasma
          separately.
        result: >-
          Interstitial adiponectin increased despite unchanged plasma adiponectin and decreased adiponectin
          mRNA. Leptin mRNA decreased.
        limitations: >-
          Interstitial concentration is not secretion rate; blood flow, recovery and compartment distribution
          matter. Acute small male sample does not define chronic response, receptor sensitivity or clinical
          benefit.
        decision: 'Supports bounded local exercise engagement, not a universal adiponectin-raising protocol.'
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: PM8-F17
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1530/EJE-07-0213'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 43
      - citation_key: moghadasi_endurance_adiponectin_2012
        doi: 10.1007/s00421-011-2073-2
        title: High-intensity endurance training improves adiponectin mRNA and plasma concentrations.
        year: '2012'
        authors:
          - Moghadasi M
          - Mohebbi H
          - Rahmani-Nia F
          - Hassan-Nia S
          - Noroozi H
          - Pirooznia N
        label: Moghadasi et al. (2012)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          16 sedentary overweight/obese men randomised 8 training/8 control; 12 weeks, 4 days/week, 45 minutes
          at 75–80% VO2max.
        endpoint: 'Abdominal/gluteal adiponectin mRNA, plasma adiponectin and body composition.'
        result: Adiponectin mRNA and plasma increased; adiposity fell concurrently.
        limitations: >-
          Transcription is not secretion or receptor responsiveness. Small sample; weight-independent
          mediation and optimal exercise dose not established.
        decision: Qualified training support; no generic secretion claim.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: PM8-F17
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1007/s00421-011-2073-2'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 44
      - citation_key: polak_aerobic_adipokines_2006
        doi: 10.1016/j.metabol.2006.06.008
        title: >-
          Effect of aerobic training on plasma levels and subcutaneous abdominal adipose tissue gene
          expression of adiponectin, leptin, interleukin 6, and tumor necrosis factor alpha in obese women.
        year: '2006'
        authors:
          - Polak J
          - Klimcakova E
          - Moro C
          - Viguerie N
          - Berlan M
          - Hejnova J
          - Richterova B
          - Kraus I
          - Langin D
          - Stich V
        label: Polak et al. (2006)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          25 sedentary women with obesity; 12-week aerobic programme, 5 days/week at 50% VO2max; biopsy
          subgroup 8.
        endpoint: Subcutaneous adipose adipokine transcripts and circulating hormones.
        result: >-
          No significant changes in tested tissue transcripts; plasma leptin fell, plasma adiponectin
          unchanged, despite weight/fat loss.
        limitations: >-
          Small biopsy subgroup; uncontrolled pre/post design. Important contextual null, not direct
          refutation of a different acute interstitial measurement.
        decision: Retain contradictory/contextual evidence; limits exercise generalisation.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered
        finding_id: PM8-F17
        source_url: 'https://doi.org/10.1016/j.metabol.2006.06.008'
        verified_on: '2026-10-26'
        reference_number: 45

    reason: >-
      Sleep candidate retained, no adipose-specific lifestyle admission. No adipose secretion, receptor or
      11β-HSD1 assay; expenditure unmeasured. Acute restriction is not proof of benefit from
      extension/regularity. Qualifies single-study sleep signal; no universal direction claimed. Secondary
      synthesis, variable energy-balance conditions and small hormone subset; no local adipose target
      measurement. Ownership distinction preserved; no PM8 admission or automatic transfer. Direct tissue
      endpoint belongs primarily to insulin responsiveness/glucose-disposal ownership; does not measure

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-niacin

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RERUN-local-cortisol
    title: Dietary modulation of local 11β-HSD1
    status: under-investigation
    question: >-
      Does this candidate engage the approved PM8 endocrine operation at its claimed exposure and evidential
      level?
    section: '3.2'
    relationship_assessment:
      type_a: Not a biochemical Capacity input.
      type_b: No protocol-specific PM8 admission established.
      delivery: Not assigned to an unadmitted intervention.
    reason: >-
      Relevant local transcript evidence; retain contrast with Tomlinson and systemic Stimson null without
      claiming all dietary engagement absent. Transcript is not local enzyme activity or cortisol-regeneration
      flux. Both diets produced weight loss; no independent effect of individual foods or weight-independent
      mechanism. Published 2015, online 2014.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      A defined exposure with appropriate PM8 endocrine measurements and supported delivery/attribution,
      preserving confounders and null evidence.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F13
    evidence_source:
      finding_ids:

    source_assessments:
      - citation_key: stomby_diet_glucocorticoid_2015
        doi: 10.1038/ijo.2014.188
        title: >-
          Diet-induced weight loss has chronic tissue-specific effects on glucocorticoid metabolism in
          overweight postmenopausal women.
        year: '2015'
        authors:
          - Stomby A
          - Simonyte K
          - Mellberg C
          - Ryberg M
          - Stimson RH
          - Larsson C
          - Lindahl B
          - Andrew R
          - Walker BR
          - Olsson T
        label: Stomby et al. (2015)
        publication_class: primary
        exposure: >-
          49 postmenopausal women with overweight/obesity randomised to Paleolithic-type or
          Nordic-recommendation diets for 24 months.
        endpoint: 'Subcutaneous adipose 11β-HSD1 mRNA, hepatic cortisone conversion and systemic metabolites.'
        result: >-
          Adipose 11β-HSD1 mRNA decreased at 6 and 24 months while weight fell; hepatic conversion increased
          transiently at 6 months.
        limitations: >-
          Transcript is not local enzyme activity or cortisol-regeneration flux. Both diets produced weight
          loss; no independent effect of individual foods or weight-independent mechanism. Published 2015,
          online 2014.
        decision: >-
          Relevant local transcript evidence; retain contrast with Tomlinson and systemic Stimson null without
          claiming all dietary engagement absent.
        access: Primary abstract and metadata; full methods not independently recovered

  - id: PM8-ADJ-NADPH-IDENTITY
    title: NADPH cofactor placement and unresolved ontology identity
    status: under-investigation
    question: >-
      Can the established endogenous reducing-equivalent role be represented by a resolved canonical
      biochemical-participant identity?
    reason: >-
      SP1 preserves the established HSD11B1 reaction. No NADPH substance record resolves in this checkout; no
      PM-specific dietary supply relationship is admitted. It does not qualify for active canonical §3.1.2
      presentation in its current state. Niacin, glucose, magnesium and foods must not be invented to fill it.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      A separately verified canonical identity and governed cofactor relationship; no dietary or food
      relationship inferred from reaction membership.
    finding_ids: []
    principle_ids:
      - PM8-SP1
    show_in_gap_summary: false
    reviewed_on: '2026-10-26'
    original_cofactors:
      - NADPH — biochemical reducing equivalent for 11β-HSD1; no dietary admission inferred
    original_main_text: >-
      NADPH participates in 11β-HSD1 reduction of cortisone to cortisol. This reaction role does not establish
      a PM-specific dietary niacin, glucose or magnesium requirement.
    history:
      - version: 1
        date: '2026-10-26'
        decision: >-
          Remove unstructured main-page entry, retain scientific provenance in SP1 and this adjudication; no
          substance or dietary admission created.
  - id: PM8-ADJ-RECOVERY-rausch_omega3_adipokines_2021
    title: >-
      Rausch et al. (2021) — Systematic review of marine-derived omega-3 fatty acid supplementation effects on
      leptin, adiponectin, and the leptin-to-adiponectin ratio.
    status: under-investigation
    question: Which exact PM8 relationship does this evidence support?
    reason: >-
      Supports recovery of primary trials and limits generalisation; no class-wide admission by vote count.
      Secondary synthesis, not 31 independent confirmations of one formulation. Primary trial overlap
      retained; dose and population variability preclude universal efficacy.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      Broader claims require target-specific measurements, independent attribution and appropriate
      replication; the bounded current disposition is not general efficacy.

      publication_class: secondary

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RECOVERY-gammelmark_fish_oil_adiponectin_2012
    title: >-
      Gammelmark et al. (2012) — Low-dose fish oil supplementation increases serum adiponectin without
      affecting inflammatory markers in overweight subjects.
    status: under-investigation
    question: Which exact PM8 relationship does this evidence support?
    reason: >-
      Oral delivery is demonstrated for the mixture. Previous blanket missing-delivery wording was too broad;
      local secretory mediation still unmeasured. Incorporation is not secretion. Mixed EPA/DHA does not
      isolate EPA versus DHA; small effect, adiposity/clearance mediation unresolved.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      Broader claims require target-specific measurements, independent attribution and appropriate
      replication; the bounded current disposition is not general efficacy.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F19
    show_in_gap_summary: false
    source_assessment:
      citation_key: gammelmark_fish_oil_adiponectin_2012
      doi: 10.1016/j.nutres.2011.12.007

        Low-dose fish oil supplementation increases serum adiponectin without affecting inflammatory markers
        in overweight subjects.

      year: '2012'
      authors:
        - Gammelmark A
        - Madsen T
        - Varming K
        - Lundbye-Christensen S
        - Schmidt EB
      publication_class: primary
      intervention: >-
        50 overweight adults randomised double-blind to 2 g/day fish oil (1.1 g marine n-3) or 2 g olive oil
        for 6 weeks; day-1 assessment.
      endpoint: Serum adiponectin and adipose/granulocyte fatty-acid incorporation; inflammatory markers.
      tissue_specificity: circulating + adipose composition
      result: >-
        Adiponectin difference +0.55 µg/mL (95% CI 0.02–1.08); adipose n-3 incorporation increased. No overall
        anti-inflammatory effect.
      limitations: >-
        Incorporation is not secretion. Mixed EPA/DHA does not isolate EPA versus DHA; small effect,
        adiposity/clearance mediation unresolved.
      prior_review: >-

      final_disposition: support bounded DHA evidence; defer EPA-specific admission

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RECOVERY-motlagh_flaxseed_adiponectin_2021
    title: >-

      adiponectin level in overweight or obese women: A randomised controlled clinical trial.
    status: admitted
    question: Which exact PM8 relationship does this evidence support?
    reason: >-
      A defined human food protocol measured an endocrine signal. Admission is limited to studied circulating
      response, not weight-independent mechanism or universal optimisation. Differential attrition,
      complete-case analysis and concurrent weight change; no constituent attribution or proven secretory
      mechanism. Adiponectin units retained as printed.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      Broader claims require target-specific measurements, independent attribution and appropriate
      replication; the bounded current disposition is not general efficacy.

      publication_class: primary
      intervention: >-

        for 12 weeks.

      tissue_specificity: 'circulating, not tissue secretion'
      result: >-

      limitations: >-

  - id: PM8-ADJ-RECOVERY-allaire_compared_2016
    title: >-
      Allaire et al. (2016) — A randomized, crossover, head-to-head comparison of eicosapentaenoic acid and
      docosahexaenoic acid supplementation to reduce inflammation markers in men and women: the Comparing EPA
      to DHA (ComparED) Study.
    status: admitted
    question: Which exact PM8 relationship does this evidence support?
    reason: >-
      DHA-specific oral exposure and endocrine signal modulation support the bounded Type B proposition. This
      is not evidence for tissue secretion or general improvement. No adipose secretion/receptor assay;
      selected population and supplement form; no ordinary-food, dose-equivalence or clinical benefit
      inferred.
    resolution_criterion: >-
      Broader claims require target-specific measurements, independent attribution and appropriate
      replication; the bounded current disposition is not general efficacy.
    finding_ids:
      - PM8-F19
    show_in_gap_summary: false
    source_assessment:
      citation_key: allaire_compared_2016
      doi: 10.3945/ajcn.116.131896
      title: >-
        A randomized, crossover, head-to-head comparison of eicosapentaenoic acid and docosahexaenoic acid
        supplementation to reduce inflammation markers in men and women: the Comparing EPA to DHA (ComparED)
        Study.
      label: Allaire et al. (2016)
      year: '2016'
      authors:
        - Allaire J
        - Couture P
        - Leclerc M
        - Charest A
        - Marin J
        - Lépine MC
        - Talbot D
        - Tchernof A
        - Lamarche B
      publication_class: primary
      intervention: >-
        154 adults randomised in double-blind crossover; 2.7 g/day EPA, 2.7 g/day DHA (90%-pure re-esterified
        triglyceride supplements), or corn oil for 10 weeks, 9-week washouts.
      endpoint: Circulating adiponectin and inflammatory/lipid endpoints.
      tissue_specificity: circulating
      result: >-
        DHA increased adiponectin more than EPA: +3.1% ±1.6% versus −1.2% ±1.7%, P<0.001 in primary abstract;
        Rausch reports DHA-control contrast.
      limitations: >-
        No adipose secretion/receptor assay; selected population and supplement form; no ordinary-food,
        dose-equivalence or clinical benefit inferred.

        This is not evidence for tissue secretion or general improvement.

      type_b: Admitted only for the bounded circulating DHA signal proposition with DHA-specific supporting trial.

## BRS6-4-8 - Adipose Endocrine Regulation

(Communicating Metabolic State Through Adipose Hormones)

## 1. Mission & Overview

### Mission

Regulate adipose-derived endocrine signalling and local glucocorticoid responsiveness to communicate metabolic state and coordinate hormonal adaptation.

**Intervention Dominance:** Diet-Supported — Context-specific dietary influence is established; no principal mission-wide intervention route selected.

### Overview

Adipose tissue communicates metabolic state through endocrine signals, including leptin and adiponectin. Hormone synthesis, release and target responsiveness respond to metabolic and hormonal conditions. Local cortisone-to-cortisol regeneration and glucocorticoid receptors provide another level of regulation. These operations interact, but they do not establish one universal causal chain: circulating hormone levels, tissue secretion and stress feedback require different measurements. [[27]](#pm-ref-27); [[28]](#pm-ref-28)

- **Biological function:** Distinct adipokines communicate with other tissues through their receptors; production and circulating concentration are not interchangeable. [[27]](#pm-ref-27)
- **Local regulation:** Human tissue studies distinguish hormone synthesis and secretion, while local cortisol conversion differs from systemic metabolism. [[30]](#pm-ref-30); [[29]](#pm-ref-29)
- **Brain relevance:** Mouse adipocyte-GR experiments support stress-feedback involvement; the mediator and corresponding human pathway remain unresolved. [[25]](#pm-ref-25)

## 2. Primary Biological Effects

- Context-dependent leptin and adiponectin output rather than universal increases or suppression. [[30]](#pm-ref-30); [[31]](#pm-ref-31)
- Local glucocorticoid availability and cellular responsiveness, distinct from systemic cortisol rhythm. [[29]](#pm-ref-29)
- A qualified peripheral contribution to stress feedback demonstrated in a genetic mouse model. [[25]](#pm-ref-25)

## 3. Intervention Levers

<span id="4-mechanistic-basis--scientific-findings" />

## 4. Mechanistic Basis + Scientific Findings

### Summary

Hormone production, release and local cortisol regeneration allow adipose tissue to respond to metabolic and hormonal conditions. These control points are related, but a change in a blood hormone concentration does not by itself identify which tissue process changed. [[30]](#pm-ref-30); [[29]](#pm-ref-29)

- **Adipokine production and release:** [synthesis, storage and secretion respond differently](#pm8-mechanism-f12), with hormone- and depot-specific effects ([SF12](#pm8-f12)).
- **Local cortisol regulation:** [adipose cortisol regeneration differs from whole-body metabolism](#pm8-mechanism-f13); enzyme expression and activity are separate endpoints ([SF13](#pm8-f13)).
- **Stress feedback:** [adipocyte glucocorticoid receptors contribute to feedback in mice](#pm8-mechanism-f14), while the mediator and equivalent human pathway remain unresolved ([SF14](#pm8-f14)).
- **Energy balance and activity:** [circulating responses](#pm8-mechanism-f15), [local hormone release during weight reduction](#pm8-mechanism-f16) and [exercise-related tissue responses](#pm8-mechanism-f17) require different interpretations ([SF15](#pm8-f15), [SF16](#pm8-f16), [SF17](#pm8-f17)).
- **EPA and DHA:** [cultured-cell secretion](#pm8-mechanism-f18) and [circulating responses to oral formulations](#pm8-mechanism-f19) establish different parts of the evidence ([SF18](#pm8-f18), [SF19](#pm8-f19)).
- **Flaxseed and soluble fibre:** [a food-specific trial signal](#pm8-mechanism-f20) and [pooled null findings](#pm8-mechanism-f21) constrain generalisation ([SF20](#pm8-f20), [SF21](#pm8-f21)).

<div class="brs-fm-hub-item" data-brs-fm-hub>
<div class="brs-fm-hub-shell">
<button type="button" class="brs-fm-hub-summary" aria-expanded="false"><span class="brs-fm-hub-chevron" aria-hidden="true"></span><strong>Adipose endocrine regulation — mechanistic detail</strong></button>
<div class="brs-fm-hub-panel" hidden>

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f12" />

#### PM8-F12

Human adipose pulse-labelling separates leptin biosynthesis from release of stored protein. Dexamethasone can increase leptin synthesis while suppressing adiponectin release in other preparations; these are distinct hormone-specific responses. Circulating adiponectin responses to systemic glucocorticoids are not uniformly reproduced. [[30]](#pm-ref-30); [[31]](#pm-ref-31); [[36]](#pm-ref-36); [[38]](#pm-ref-38)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F12](#pm8-f12). Foundational hormone identities: [PM8-SP2](?review=1#pm8-sp2).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f13" />

#### PM8-F13

Microdialysis and tracer measurements distinguish local cortisone-to-cortisol conversion from systemic turnover. Insulin effects differed by adiposity. A low-carbohydrate crossover altered systemic cortisol metabolism without a measured adipose 11β-HSD1 response. Weight-loss studies reported different directions of adipose transcript change: an increase in one small study and decreases at six and 24 months in another. Neither transcript direction establishes a matching change in local cortisol regeneration. [[29]](#pm-ref-29); [[32]](#pm-ref-32); [[33]](#pm-ref-33); [[48]](#pm-ref-48)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F13](#pm8-f13). Established reaction provenance: [PM8-SP1 — 11β-HSD1](?review=1#pm8-sp1).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f14" />

#### PM8-F14

The genetic experiment supports a qualified contribution of adipocyte-enriched GR signalling to mouse stress feedback. The communicating signal was not identified; unchanged baseline adipokines do not establish an adipokine-mediated pathway. Human stress feedback through the same route remains unresolved. [[25]](#pm-ref-25)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F14](#pm8-f14).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f15" />

#### PM8-F15

Alternate-day and daily energy restriction reduced circulating leptin without an adiponectin change in one randomised trial. POUNDS Lost reported increased circulating adiponectin over two years across diet groups. A small walking trial increased adipose adiponectin mRNA without a significant plasma increase. Duration, tissue measurement and circulating exposure must remain separate. [[34]](#pm-ref-34); [[35]](#pm-ref-35); [[37]](#pm-ref-37)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F15](#pm8-f15).


<span id="pm8-mechanism-f16" />

#### PM8-F16

Energy deficit and weight reduction can alter adipose hormone release. Hypocaloric programmes in women with obesity reduced measured leptin release, while adiponectin responses differed by depot and phase or remained unchanged. Energy deficit, weight loss and accompanying changes cannot be fully separated in these results. [[39]](#pm-ref-39); [[40]](#pm-ref-40); [[41]](#pm-ref-41)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F16](#pm8-f16).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f17" />

#### PM8-F17

Exercise can change the local adipose endocrine environment without producing the same change in circulating hormones. Human studies measured acute interstitial adiponectin changes and training-related gene-expression responses, alongside null tissue findings in another population. Interstitial concentration is not secretion rate, and messenger RNA is not released protein. [[43]](#pm-ref-43); [[44]](#pm-ref-44); [[45]](#pm-ref-45)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F17](#pm8-f17).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f18" />

#### PM8-F18

Defined EPA and DHA exposures increased adiponectin secretion in cultured human adipocytes. Oral fish-oil trials measured heterogeneous circulating responses; they do not establish that ordinary foods reproduce the cultured-cell exposure or secretory mechanism. [[46]](#pm-ref-46); [[47]](#pm-ref-47)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F18](#pm8-f18).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f19" />

#### PM8-F19

Selected oral DHA formulations changed circulating adiponectin in human interventions. Mixed marine-omega-3 and DHA-enriched-oil studies provide related but formulation-specific evidence. Adipose fatty-acid incorporation and a blood hormone response do not establish altered local secretion, receptor sensitivity or equivalent effects from ordinary fish intake. [[68]](#pm-ref-68); [[64]](#pm-ref-64); [[65]](#pm-ref-65); [[63]](#pm-ref-63)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F19](#pm8-f19).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f20" />

#### PM8-F20

A milled-flaxseed intervention reported a circulating adiponectin difference in overweight or obese women. The broader pooled evidence was heterogeneous and did not establish an overall adiponectin effect. The food-specific result does not identify ALA, lignans or fibre as the causal constituent, nor establish weight-independent mediation or altered adipose secretion. [[66]](#pm-ref-66); [[69]](#pm-ref-69)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F20](#pm8-f20).

<span id="pm8-mechanism-f21" />

#### PM8-F21

Pooled soluble-fibre trials did not establish significant overall changes in circulating leptin or adiponectin and showed substantial heterogeneity. This limits a generic soluble-fibre endocrine claim; it does not establish the absence of every preparation-specific effect or identify a local adipose mechanism. [[67]](#pm-ref-67)

Supporting evidence: [PM8-F21](#pm8-f21).

#### (Boundaries of the mechanism)

Adipokine output and local adipose glucocorticoid regulation are distinct from [insulin-responsive glucose disposal](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm1/brs6-fm1-pm3-insulin-sensitivity-and-glucose-disposal), [systemic cortisol timing](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm2/brs6-fm2-pm4-cortisol-rhythm-regulation) and [central stress-modulated appetite and reward](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm4/brs6-fm4-pm9-stress-induced-appetite-reward-drive-modulation). Inflammatory activation and resolution remain within [BRS3](/docs/biological-targets/inflammation-oxidative-stress). Reduced fat-depot size alone is not an endocrine endpoint.

#### (Integration within BRS6)

Adipose endocrine output provides the peripheral component of [metabolic–endocrine signalling and adaptive regulation](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm4/brs6-fm4-stress-inflammation-metabolic-load-allocation). PM9 addresses central appetite and reward responses. No shared Key Constraint relationship is currently admitted; this placement does not establish a single adipokine–glucocorticoid–brain causal chain.

</div>
</div>
</div>

### 4.1 Scientific Findings

#### Summary

Human tissue, local cortisol and mouse stress-feedback evidence address distinct endocrine operations. Intervention measurements do not establish a single adipokine–glucocorticoid–brain causal chain.

<ScientificFinding finding={{"id":"PM8-F15","finding_label":"Diet and activity outcomes distinguish circulating hormones from local production","presentation":"mechanistic-basis","fm_rollup":false,"finding_summary":"Dietary energy restriction and physical activity can change selected circulating adipokines or adipose transcripts, but these endpoints do not establish a uniform change in adipokine secretion or improved endocrine responsiveness.","finding_statement":"Dietary energy restriction and physical activity can change selected circulating adipokines or adipose transcripts, but these endpoints do not establish a uniform change in adipokine secretion or improved endocrine responsiveness.","finding_interpretation":"Alternate-day and daily energy restriction reduced circulating leptin without an adiponectin change in one randomised trial. POUNDS Lost reported increased circulating adiponectin over two years across diet groups. A small walking trial increased adipose adiponectin mRNA without a significant plasma increase. Duration, tissue measurement and circulating exposure must remain separate.","synthesis":"Alternate-day and daily energy restriction reduced circulating leptin without an adiponectin change in one randomised trial. POUNDS Lost reported increased circulating adiponectin over two years across diet groups. A small walking trial increased adipose adiponectin mRNA without a significant plasma increase. Duration, tissue measurement and circulating exposure must remain separate.","synthesis_limitations":"These trials do not directly quantify adipose secretion or receptor sensitivity. Lower leptin or higher adiponectin is not automatically a better endocrine state. Selected populations, adherence and weight change limit translation; no optimal universal protocol or brain outcome is established.","synthesised_evidence_confidence":"not-yet-scored","evidence_considered":[{"label":"Trepanowski et al. <ScientificFinding finding={{"id":"PM8-F16","finding_label":"Dietary weight-reduction programmes alter local adipokine release within studied contexts","presentation":"mechanistic-basis","fm_rollup":false,"finding_summary":"Hypocaloric weight-reduction programmes in women with obesity reduced ex vivo adipose leptin release, while adiponectin responses were depot-specific or unchanged.","finding_statement":"Hypocaloric weight-reduction programmes in women with obesity reduced ex vivo adipose leptin release, while adiponectin responses were depot-specific or unchanged.","finding_interpretation":"Independent programmes measured adipose release rather than relying on circulating concentrations alone. You measured release in 27 of 42 women and found abdominal but not gluteal adiponectin increases. Arvidsson found lower leptin secretion without an adiponectin increase. Siklova shows that active restriction and stabilisation differ. Fasting and mixed-meal arteriovenous studies provide contextual evidence of acute local regulation, not equivalent weight-loss protocols.","synthesis":"Independent programmes measured adipose release rather than relying on circulating concentrations alone. You measured release in 27 of 42 women and found abdominal but not gluteal adiponectin increases. Arvidsson found lower leptin secretion without an adiponectin increase. Siklova shows that active restriction and stabilisation differ. Fasting and mixed-meal arteriovenous studies provide contextual evidence of acute local regulation, not equivalent weight-loss protocols.","synthesis_limitations":"Energy deficit, weight loss and co-interventions are not fully separable. Ex vivo release, venous–arterial concentration difference, transcript and plasma measurements remain distinct. <ScientificFinding finding={{"id":"PM8-F17","finding_label":"Aerobic exercise engages local adipokine regulation with compartment-specific and null responses","presentation":"mechanistic-basis","fm_rollup":false,"finding_summary":"Aerobic exercise changed adipose interstitial adiponectin or adipokine transcripts in some human protocols, with null tissue or circulating responses in other studied populations.","finding_statement":"Aerobic exercise changed adipose interstitial adiponectin or adipokine transcripts in some human protocols, with null tissue or circulating responses in other studied populations.","finding_interpretation":"Acute exercise increased local interstitial adiponectin while plasma stayed unchanged and mRNA decreased. These are different measurements and contexts, not independent replications of one secretion-rate effect.","synthesis":"Acute exercise increased local interstitial adiponectin while plasma stayed unchanged and mRNA decreased. A small training study increased adipose adiponectin mRNA; another training study found no changes in tested tissue transcripts. These are different measurements and contexts, not independent replications of one secretion-rate effect.","synthesis_limitations":"Interstitial concentration is not secretion flux, and mRNA is not released protein. <p><a href="#pm8-mechanism-f21">Mechanistic explanation: Soluble-fibre pooled adipokine evidence does not establish a generic endocrine intervention</a></p>

## 5. BRS Pathways and Connections

### 5.1 BRS Pathways

Peripheral endocrine communication contributes to metabolic–endocrine adaptation. No whole-body resource-allocation mechanism is inferred.

### 5.2 Cross-BRS Mechanism Relationships

BRS3 inflammatory regulation can influence adipose endocrine conditions, but inflammation is not this PM’s defining operation. No new causal graph or brain-outcome relationship is admitted.

### 5.3 Local BRS Mechanism Relationships

[Insulin Sensitivity & Glucose Disposal](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm1/brs6-fm1-pm3-insulin-sensitivity-and-glucose-disposal) addresses insulin-responsive disposal; [Cortisol Rhythm Regulation](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm2/brs6-fm2-pm4-cortisol-rhythm-regulation) addresses systemic timing. [Stress-Modulated Appetite & Reward Regulation](/docs/biological-targets/brs6/fm4/brs6-fm4-pm9-stress-induced-appetite-reward-drive-modulation) addresses central appetite/reward integration, not adipokine production.

## 7. Phenome Connections

These mappings are translational relationships, not single-mechanism outcome claims. Phenomes are emergent functional patterns supported by multiple interacting PMs across the BRAIN Framework.
